# Development harness

The mod ships a development-only command channel so a shell script can run a load-and-check cycle against the running game without simulating keyboard or mouse input: load a level, capture the game's own rendered frame, and quit. It is driven by `scripts/run.py`.

For the separate **megabonkmod.party** Astro showcase, see [Website development](website.md) for local commands, content and media maintenance, validation and deployment boundaries.

## Enabling

The harness is off by default. It runs only if the marker file `<game dir>/harness/enabled` exists when the game starts, so a normal install exposes no command channel.

```sh
uv run scripts/run.py enable    # creates harness/enabled and harness/commands
uv run scripts/run.py disable   # removes the marker
```

The marker gates only the file queue: without it the queue folders are not created or polled, but the logger is still set, so command handlers work. `DevHarness.Run(HarnessCommand)` is the in-process entry point (main thread only): it runs one parsed command and returns the same `{command, ok, result|error}` envelope the queue writes, turning a handler exception into `ok=false` with the message. The queue calls it too.

Enabling takes effect on the next game launch. When active, the mod logs `DevHarness: enabled, watching '<dir>'` to `BepInEx/LogOutput.log`.

`scripts/game_paths.py` reports `bepinex_dir`, `plugins_dir`, `plugin_dir`, `latest_log_path` and `wine_log_path` alongside the game directory and Wine prefix. The deploy folder is `BepInEx/plugins/MegabonkLevels/`, the loader log is `BepInEx/LogOutput.log`, and plain-Wine launch diagnostics are in `BepInEx/wine-latest.log`. The file queue remains at `<game>/harness/`, with console history and content in shared `UserData/`.

After changing these scripts, restart any already-connected MCP server to reload its Python modules; otherwise its log helpers can still point at the old loader log. A fresh server's `paths()` must report `BepInEx/LogOutput.log` as `latest_log`.

The game directory is resolved the same way as in `scripts/game_paths.py`: process environment, then the nearest `.env` found walking up from the current directory or the script, then the built-in default: `<prefix>/drive_c/Program Files (x86)/Steam/steamapps/common/Megabonk` inside the Wine prefix on macOS, `C:\Program Files (x86)\Steam\steamapps\common\Megabonk` on Windows. The keys are `MEGABONK_GAME_DIR` (the game directory itself, any platform), `MEGABONK_WINE_PREFIX` (the Wine prefix, the directory that contains `drive_c`) and `MEGABONK_BOTTLE` (the older spelling: the prefix's `drive_c`; `MEGABONK_WINE_PREFIX` wins when both are set). The default prefix is `~/.local/share/megabonk/wine` for plain Wine and the CrossOver Steam bottle (`~/Library/Application Support/CrossOver/Bottles/Steam`) for the CrossOver runtime. `MEGABONK_WINE_PREFIX` also applies when `MEGABONK_GAME_DIR` points outside the prefix, which is how the launch scripts and `wine:dxvk` find the prefix in that case; without either setting the prefix is the `drive_c` ancestor of the game directory. `uv run scripts/game_paths.py` prints the resolved `game_dir` and `wine_prefix`. On macOS, `MEGABONK_MACOS_RUNTIME` (`wine`, the default, or `crossover`) picks the runtime and `MEGABONK_WINE` points at the plain Wine binary.

## Commands

`run.py` writes one JSON file per command into `<game dir>/harness/commands/`. The mod polls that folder twice a second, runs the files in name order, first moves each to `harness/processing/` so it cannot replay after a crash or quit, then moves it to `harness/done/` on success or `harness/rejected/` when parsing or execution fails.

| Command | Wire format | `run.py` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Load a level and start the run | `{"command":"load-level","path":"<file>"}` | `run.py load <level> [--map N] [--stage N] [--tier N] [--seed N] [--no-seed]` |
| Load an AssetBundle and read back its assets (see [asset-bundles.md](asset-bundles.md)) | `{"command":"load-bundle","path":"<bundle>"}` | `run.py bundle <bundle>` |
| List the shaders the game ships and the scene materials use (see [asset-bundles.md](asset-bundles.md)) | `{"command":"shader-report"}` | `run.py shader-report` |
| List the shader, keywords, textures and colors of every material under the objects whose name contains the argument, default Warehouse (see [asset-bundles.md](asset-bundles.md)) | `{"command":"material-dump","object":"<name>"}` | `run.py material-dump [object]` |
| Save the rendered frame as a PNG | `{"command":"screenshot","path":"<file>"}` | `run.py screenshot <out.png>` |
| Freeze gameplay | `{"command":"freeze"}` | `run.py freeze` |
| Resume gameplay | `{"command":"resume"}` | `run.py resume` |
| Make the player survive enemy damage | `{"command":"invulnerable"}` | `run.py invulnerable` |
| Restore normal player damage | `{"command":"vulnerable"}` | `run.py vulnerable` |
| Apply 1–99 integer damage, preserving 1 HP by default | `{"command":"damage","amount":37,"nonlethal":true}` | `run.py damage 37` |
| Control level music: play, pause, next, prev, track, list, now (see "Music transport") | `{"command":"music","action":"track","track":12}` | `run.py music track 12` |
| Kill the nearest enemy, all enemies or all bosses (see "Killing enemies") | `{"command":"kill-enemies","target":"bosses"}` | `run.py kill-enemies bosses` |
| End the run at once by killing the player (ignores invulnerability) | `{"command":"kill"}` | `run.py kill` |
| Run the game's own restart, the death screen's Retry button | `{"command":"restart-run"}` | `run.py restart-run` |
| Show or hide the stock HUD | `{"command":"hud","enabled":false}` | `run.py hud <on\|off>` |
| Independently show or hide coordinates | `{"command":"coordinates","enabled":true}` | `run.py coordinates <on\|off>` (also F7) |
| Allow or stop new enemy spawns | `{"command":"spawns","enabled":false}` | `run.py spawns <on\|off>` |
| Check in-game Steamworks readiness | `{"command":"steam-ready"}` | `run.py steam-ready` |
| Read the game's current state | `{"command":"state"}` | `run.py state` |
| Run a console line without opening the overlay | `{"command":"console","line":"help player"}` | `run.py console 'help player'` |
| Start or stop the kill-floor heartbeat | `{"command":"heartbeat","enabled":true}` | `run.py heartbeat <on\|off>` |
| Start or stop the cell-change log | `{"command":"cells","enabled":true}` | `run.py cells <on\|off>` |
| Enter or leave editor mode | `{"command":"editor","enabled":true}` | `run.py editor <on\|off>` |
| Hold the camera at a pose | `{"command":"camera","position":[x,y,z],"rotation":[rx,ry,rz]}` | `run.py camera X Y Z --rot RX RY RZ` |
| Return the camera to the game | `{"command":"camera-clear"}` | `run.py camera --clear` |
| Tap, press or release a key | `{"command":"key","action":"tap","key":"R","modifiers":["LeftShift"]}` | `run.py key R --mod LeftShift`, `run.py key --down W`, `run.py key --up W` |
| Move, click, press or release the mouse | `{"command":"mouse","action":"click","button":0,"x":960,"y":540}` | `run.py mouse move X Y`, `run.py mouse click [--button N] [X Y]`, `run.py mouse down\|up`, `run.py mouse scroll --delta N [X Y]`, `run.py mouse clear` |
| List the editor widgets, or find the one at a pixel | `{"command":"ui-tree"}` or `{"command":"ui-tree","x":120,"y":35}` | `run.py ui-tree [X Y]` |
| Click an editor widget by name | `{"command":"click-widget","name":"Save"}` | `run.py click-widget Save` |
| Save the editor's level, optionally under a name | `{"command":"save-level"}` or `{"command":"save-level","level":"my_level"}` | `run.py save-level [name]` |
| Write a blank from-scratch level and open it in the editor | `{"command":"new-level"}` or `{"command":"new-level","level":"arena","map":1,"stageIndex":0,"tier":0,"size":7}` | `run.py new-level [name] [--map N] [--stage N] [--tier N] [--size N]` |
| List the saved custom-level files | `{"command":"list-levels"}` | `run.py list-levels` |
| List every palette entry for the loaded map | `{"command":"palette"}` | `run.py palette` |
| List loaded Unity objects of a type with chosen fields | `{"command":"query-objects","type":"MapData","fields":["eMap"]}` or `{"command":"query-objects","type":"MapData","fields":["stages"],"depth":2}` | `run.py query-objects <TYPE> [--fields F ...] [--depth N]`, MCP `query_objects` |
| Type text | `{"command":"type","text":"chest"}` | `run.py type chest` |
| Read or set a player item amount | `{"command":"item","name":"Mirror"}` or `{"command":"item","name":"Mirror","amount":3}` | `run.py item Mirror`, `run.py item Mirror 3` |
| Profile frame time over a measurement window (see "Frame-time profiling") | `{"command":"perf","action":"start"}`, `{"action":"report"}`, `{"action":"status"}`, `{"action":"reset"}`, `{"action":"spawn","count":120}`, `{"action":"timescale","value":4}`, `{"action":"toggle","target":"KillFloor","enabled":false}`, `{"action":"vsync","enabled":false}`, `{"action":"health","hp":1000000}`, `{"action":"render","enabled":false}`, `{"action":"physics","enabled":false}`, `{"action":"animation","enabled":false}` | `run.py perf start\|report\|status\|reset`, `perf spawn --count N`, `perf timescale [--value X]`, `perf toggle --target NAME --enable\|--disable`, `perf vsync --disable`, `perf health [--hp N]`, `perf render --enable\|--disable`, `perf physics --enable\|--disable`, `perf animation --enable\|--disable` |
| Hold or release the slide button the grind gate reads (see "Grind gate") | `{"command":"grind-slide","held":true}` or `{"command":"grind-slide"}` | `run.py grind-slide <on\|off\|auto>` |
| Read the live player's fall-damage inputs (see "Fall damage") | `{"command":"fall-damage"}` | `run.py fall-damage` |
| Move the player | `{"command":"teleport","position":[x,y,z]}` or `{"command":"teleport","cell":[cx,cz]}` | `run.py teleport X Y Z`, `run.py teleport --cell CX CY` |
| Pick an upgrade in the open level-up dialog | `{"command":"dismiss-levelup","choice":0}` | `run.py dismiss-levelup [--choice N]` |
| Close, pick or skip the open reward window (level-up, shrine offers, chest, microwave) | `{"command":"dismiss-offers"}` / `{"command":"dismiss-offers","choice":N}` / `{"command":"dismiss-offers","skip":true}` | `run.py dismiss-offers [--choice N] [--skip]` |
| Activate the nearest interactable through the game's own `Interact` (see "Interactables") | `{"command":"interact"}` / `{"command":"interact","type":"Magnet","maxDistance":8,"force":true}` | `run.py interact [--type SUBSTRING] [--max-distance N] [--force]` |
| Decode or audition runtime mp3 PCM (see "Runtime music probe") | `{"command":"music-probe","action":"play","path":"01 Police Truck.mp3"}` | `run.py music-probe <action> [path] [--variant il2cpp\|managed] [--value N]` |
| Quit the game | `{"command":"quit"}` | `run.py quit [--stop-steam] [--stop-menu]` |
| Relaunch the test level in editor mode | Multiple commands | `run.py relaunch [level] [--map N] [--stage N] [--tier N] [--seed N] [--no-seed] [--stop-steam] [--stop-menu]` |
| Run the quit teardown with no harness marker and no running game | — | `run.py teardown [--stop-steam] [--stop-menu]` |

`load-level` takes optional integer fields `map`, `stageIndex`, `tier` and `seed` that replace those fields of the level file for that load only. The mod merges them in memory after reading the file, so the file on disk is not modified and no temporary level file exists. `run.py load` and `run.py relaunch` spell them `--map`, `--stage`, `--tier` and `--seed`; the MCP `load` and `relaunch` tools take `map`, `stage`, `tier` and `seed`. `map` is an `eMap` value as listed in `level-format.md` (Forest 1, Desert 2, Graveyard 8, Dungeon 16, Hell 64). A value that is not an integer, or a `map` that is not one of those values, is rejected when the command is parsed, naming the field; the CLI and MCP reject a non-integer before sending anything. A `stage` outside the chosen map's stage range is rejected by the loader with `LevelLoader: load failed: stageIndex N out of range for <map> (M stages)` in the game log, which `relaunch` reports as a failed load, with that log line in its error. Without overrides the level file is loaded as written.

`"seed": null` in the command (spelled `--no-seed` on the CLI and `unseeded=true` on the MCP tools) is the inverse of `seed`: the mod leaves seeding to the game instead of pinning `RunConfig.levelSeed`, so the run gets the seed the game rolls for itself. It cannot be combined with `seed`, and it shows up as `SeedForcePatch mode -> Off (seed left to the game)` followed by `LevelLoader: starting ... seed=game-rolled`; the `A_GenerationComplete` line then carries the game's own `debugSeed` rather than the level's.

For stage measurements only, launch with both `MEGABONK_DEV=true` and `MEGABONK_DEV_STAGE_OVERRIDE=true` to make the initial generation use the requested `stageIndex` (`--stage` on `load`/`relaunch`). The second switch is default-off and is explicitly forwarded through the macOS GUI launcher. Vanilla `StartNewMap` otherwise resets `MapController.index` to -1 and calls `LoadNextStage`, which selects `currentMap.stages[++index]` regardless of `RunConfig.stageData`. The switch supplies `stageIndex - 1` just before that first increment, consumes the request once, and does not redirect later portals or lift the custom-level tier cap; this is a stage-asset measurement, not a stock Tier 2 playthrough. The log says `development initial stage override -> N`. After each fresh launch, verify the actual assets with `query-objects ProceduralTileGeneration --fields currentStage currentMapParameters --depth 2`; for Desert, whose tile generator is unused, query `MapController --fields index currentStage runConfig --depth 2` and `MapGenerator --fields terrainData noiseData --depth 2` instead. `MapGenerationController.testStageData` is a serialized test default, not its effective stage. Remove the switch for normal play; see [the effective-stage measurements](runtime-map-structure.md#12-tier-2-stage-identity-and-per-stage-generation-parameters-measured).

For terrain measurements only, launch with both `MEGABONK_DEV=true` and `MEGABONK_DEV_TERRAIN_SEED=true` to hold the terrain heightmap still across runs. `MapGenerationController`'s generation coroutine rolls `MapGenerationController.mapSeed` with `Random.Range(0, 0x7FFFFFFF)` and passes it to `MapGenerator.GenerateMap(MapData, StageData, int)` (RVA `0x509EA0`), which forwards it to `GeneratePerlinNoiseMap` and then to `Noise.GenerateNoiseMap`; nothing reads a seed from `RunConfig`. With the switch on, a seeded harness load replaces that one argument with the level's seed, and an unseeded load (`--no-seed`) arms nothing and only observes what the game rolled. The replacement is a Harmony prefix that logs both values and calls the method itself with the pinned seed, skipping the original under a re-entrancy guard; assigning to the parameter in place was tried first and corrupted the call, which surfaced as an `IndexOutOfRangeException` inside `MeshGenerator.GenerateTerrainMesh` that appears in no unpatched log. Tile and object seeding are untouched: `SeedForcePatch` still pins `ProceduralTileGeneration.debugSeed` for the tile phase, and the switch never changes what a normal launch without it does. It is consumed by the first generation after a load, so a second generation in the same process uses the game's own roll.

The switch reports four kinds of evidence. `TerrainSeed: armed terrain seed N for the next generation` records the request. `TerrainSeed: pinned seed=N (the game rolled M) stage=<StageData name>` records the replacement. `TerrainSeed: noise seed=N scale=… octaves=… persistance=… lacunarity=… blend=… blendStrength=…` verifies the integer reaching the pure noise function (`Noise.GenerateNoiseMap`, RVA `0x51CED0`), which seeds `System.Random` per octave. `TerrainSeed: meshArgument hash=<sha256> cells=<n> read=<how> ptr=<address> min=… max=… mean=…` hashes the actual grid passed to `MeshGenerator.GenerateTerrainMesh`, with separate `heightMap` and `staticNoiseMap` field-read attempts as corroboration. The SHA-256 input is invariant four-decimal float text with comma separators, not raw float bytes. `cells@multi` or `cells@sz` means the bulk copy agrees with independently sampled cells; `buffer@multi` or `buffer@sz` means only plausibility checks ran (finite magnitudes at most 1e7, with variation). No cell-by-cell validation succeeded in the recorded matrix. `none` or `unreadable:<type>` means no grid was read. The candidate il2cpp array headers place count/data at offsets 24/32 for `multi`, 16/24 for `sz`; `ptr=` distinguishes array addresses. `A_GenerationComplete`'s `mapSeed` remains the game's own roll because the patch replaces the argument, not the field. Remove the switch for normal play; see [the terrain seed measurements](runtime-map-structure.md#the-terrain-seed-under-control-measured).

`run.py cycle <level> <out.png>` runs load, waits `--settle` seconds (default 5), takes the screenshot, then quits. `--invulnerable` makes the player invulnerable right after the load, before the settle wait. `--freeze` freezes gameplay and `--camera X Y Z RX RY RZ` poses the camera after the settle wait and before the screenshot. `--timeout` (before the subcommand, default 15 seconds) sets how long to wait for each command and for the PNG to appear. The exit code is 0 only if every step succeeded, 1 if a step failed or timed out, and 2 if the harness is not enabled (`run.py` prints `harness not enabled at <dir>; run 'run.py enable' and relaunch the game`).

On every platform, every quit (`run.py quit`, `run.py teardown`, and the quit inside `relaunch` and `cycle`) also stops the processes a game leaves behind: Wine's session stubs (`services.exe`, `plugplay.exe`, `rpcss.exe`, `svchost.exe`, `winedevice.exe`, `conhost.exe`, `explorer.exe /desktop`, `tabtip.exe`, `lsass.exe`, `winemenubuilder.exe`), which otherwise pile up as extra MelonLoader console windows; orphaned game helpers such as `UnityCrashHandler64.exe`, which the game leaves running after its own process exits; and Steam's own launcher wrappers for app id 3405340 (`reaper SteamLaunch AppId=3405340`, `srt-bwrap`, `pv-adverb`), which hold a Proton prefix open after the game dies. The stop is a `SIGTERM`, but a Wine stub whose `wineserver` died first never processes it — a `winedevice.exe` in that state sits on forever — so after a 1.5-second grace the cleanup `SIGKILL`s whatever is still running (`quit: force-killed N stubborn Wine processes`). Leftover stubs make the next boot race stale Wine plumbing, one way a fresh launch can die seconds after MelonLoader's `0 Plugins loaded` line, and a Linux box whose quit skipped this step accumulated dozens of stubs and a `(not responding)` console window per run. A leftover `wineserver` is not killed by the quit; `relaunch` waits for it to exit (killing it after a short grace) before launching, so a fresh session never races the previous one's teardown — a relaunch into a prefix that was not fully torn down used to die several seconds into boot, right after MelonLoader's `0 Plugins loaded` line.

Only processes belonging to the resolved prefix are killed. A process is ours when its `WINEPREFIX` names that prefix, when its `STEAM_COMPAT_DATA_PATH` names the directory holding it (Proton exports that instead of `WINEPREFIX`, so it is the signal a Steam-launched run actually leaves behind), when one of its argv entries contains a `drive_c` or `pfx` root that resolves to it, or when one of its open files does (on macOS the CrossOver stubs have no bottle in argv, but they hold files under `Bottles/<name>/drive_c`, which one `lsof -n -P -F pn -p <pids>` call reads for the Windows processes in the single `ps` table function; the Linux `/proc` path execs nothing); a process with none of those signals takes the bottle that every attributed process sharing its wineserver socket directory (`/private/tmp/.wine-<uid>/server-<id>/`) agrees on, and stays unattributed when that directory holds two bottles; anything attributed to another prefix is never touched, so a second prefix — another game, another Proton version — survives a quit. Even our own stubs wait while another live Windows process shares the prefix, which is what keeps the cleanup from pulling Wine's services out from under a Steam client that itself runs inside it (the Wine-under-CrossOver layout). A process nothing can attribute waits unless no Windows process is live at all, the old blanket rule kept for exactly that case (`lsof` missing or refused, or no bottle signal at all). A live Windows process in a foreign bottle therefore no longer holds back stubs attributed to ours. When anything is held back the quit says so and names each spared process, one per line: `quit: leaving Wine leftovers alone while other Windows processes run`, then `quit:   pid <pid> <executable> bottle=<bottle or unknown>: <reason>`, where the reason is `live Windows process in this bottle`, `foreign bottle, never touched` or `no bottle signal while Windows processes run`. The Steam client is never a candidate: it is the basename `steam` or `steam.sh` in argv[0], or the Flatpak id `com.valvesoftware.Steam` anywhere in argv, so a Linux quit clears its own stubs with the client up. The `steam.exe` Proton runs inside the prefix for the overlay is a different thing: it names the game's own directory in its argv, so it is reaped as a game helper, which is right — the steamruntime launcher starts it for that run, so it is part of what the run leaves behind.

Everything the harness needs to know about live processes comes from one provider rather than parsed shell text. On Linux that is `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` and `/proc/<pid>/environ`, both NUL-separated, so the backslashes and spaces in a `C:\Program Files\…` path cannot corrupt field splitting and the environment is available for attribution; kernel threads and zombies have an empty `cmdline` and are skipped. Everywhere else it is one `ps -axo pid=,args=` with `LC_ALL=C` and the header suppressed — `pid` and `args` being the two keywords the BSD and the GNU family both accept, and a pinned locale keeping the columns predictable — with no environment, which is what makes attribution fall back to the blanket rule. Windows keeps its `tasklist /FO CSV` branch. Nothing runs `pgrep` or `pkill`: candidates are matched by exact, casefolded Windows basename over argv, so `C:\games\svchost_my.exe` is not a stub and `tabtip.exe` is, and a signal is only ever sent to a pid the same call listed.

The Steam client and the OS-level menu input service (`run.py menu start`, see [gamepadui.md](gamepadui.md)) outlive a run on purpose, because a relaunch needs both, so they are torn down only when asked for: `--stop-steam` and `--stop-menu` on `quit`, `relaunch` and `teardown`, or `task game:stop -- --stop-steam --stop-menu`. `--stop-steam` asks the client down through `steam steam://shutdownsteam`, or `flatpak run com.valvesoftware.Steam steam://shutdownsteam` where only the Flatpak exists, waits for it, then `SIGTERM`s the pids it listed and allows 10 seconds before `SIGKILL`ing survivors — deliberately not the Wine stub's 1.5-second grace, because a client that is saving state needs longer and was being killed outright. It reports `stop-steam: Steam is not running` when nothing was listed, and `stop-steam: stopped N Steam process(es) after SIGTERM` when the signals did the work. Expect the request itself to be ignored on the current Linux client: like the gamescope session noted in [gamepadui.md](gamepadui.md), it prints its confirmation and does nothing, so the `SIGTERM` is what actually lands. Two more caveats from that host: the gamescope session brings the client straight back, and `--stop-steam` on a `relaunch` means the launch that follows starts Steam again, so the next run pays for the client's logon. `run.py teardown` is the same teardown with no harness marker and no running game required, for clearing a box that is already littered; `menu: input service not running` is a success, so `--stop-menu` is safe to repeat.

A game can also be alive but unable to answer: the harness polls from `ModTick.Update` on the Unity main thread, and a window macOS has suspended (typically an editor left idle for a long time) stops that thread, so a `quit` sits in `harness/commands/` unprocessed until it times out. `quit` escalates in that case: after the command times out, if the process is still running it force-kills every process running out of the game directory (`quit: force-killed N game processes`) and waits again. Only a game that survives even the force kill is reported as a failed quit. Because a timed-out command stays in the queue and a fresh session would run it at boot (a stale `quit` would quit the new run), `relaunch` empties `commands/`, `processing/`, `done/`, `rejected/` and `results/` before launching.

`run.py relaunch` quits the running game, waits for the previous session's `wineserver` to exit (killing it after a 15-second grace if it lingers, printing `relaunch: force-killed N lingering wineserver(s)` and `SIGKILL`ing any Wine stub that outlives it), empties the harness queue, starts a fresh process through `task game:run` (`task crossover:run` when `MEGABONK_MACOS_RUNTIME=crossover`), waits for the new session's `DevHarness: enabled` log entry — the log must have been written after the launch, so the previous session's line cannot satisfy the wait — then polls the in-game Steamworks client before loading `levels/example-forest.mblevel.json`. Both waits also watch the game process: once the startup grace passes, a missing Megabonk process ends the relaunch at once with `relaunch: game exited during startup (see <LogOutput.log>)` instead of waiting out the timeout. That error first copies the dead boot's logs — `BepInEx/wine-latest.log`, `BepInEx/LogOutput.log`, and the two Unity `Player*.log` files — into `~/Library/Logs/megabonk-relaunch-failures/<timestamp>/` (`MEGABONK_EVIDENCE_DIR` overrides the root) and appends `evidence saved to <dir>`, because every later launch rotates `wine-latest.log` over its `.prev` and destroys the only copy. Every relaunch failure then force-kills any game process and reaps the Wine leftovers, so a failed relaunch leaves no wedged game or stale stub behind for the next attempt. A Steamworks client that answers `ready:false` and keeps answering — the offline case on the Steam stub, where the game's `SteamManager` has already failed init and will not retry — is accepted after a 15-second grace with the step `relaunch: Steamworks not ready (offline run); continuing`; only a client that never answers at all fails with `relaunch: Steamworks did not become ready`. It retries the load until `KillFloor: armed` appears, then enables editor mode. The player gets 2 Feathers (extra jumps) through the `item` command; `MEGABONK_RELAUNCH_FEATHERS` sets another amount from 0 to 999, and 0 leaves them alone. Enemy spawns stay on and the player takes normal damage by default. Set `MEGABONK_RELAUNCH_SPAWNS=false` in the environment or `.env` to stop spawns after the load (see "Enemy spawns") and `MEGABONK_RELAUNCH_INVULNERABLE=true` to make the player invulnerable (see "Player invulnerability"); `task` loads `.env` through the `dotenv` directive in `taskfile.yml`, so `task game:editor` sees both. The MCP `spawns` and `invulnerable` tools and `run.py spawns` and `run.py invulnerable` change either at runtime. It allows at least 60 seconds for readiness checks, even when `--timeout` is lower. Pass an optional level path to use a different level file. `--no-level` (MCP `relaunch(no_level=true)`) stops at the main menu once Steamworks is ready, with no level loaded and no editor, for checking the stock menu screens. Use this command between interactive editor sessions; do not send another `load` to an existing run. A second `load` while in editor mode is survivable: `LiveEditPatch` hides its session while the old tile generator is destroyed, so the editor tools find no grid until the reloaded level finishes generating, and the editor re-enters on the new run.

Neither the plain-Wine launcher nor the CrossOver `cxstart` task passes MelonLoader's `--melonloader.agfoffline` switch. BepInEx needs network for its first interop generation and reuses its own generated assemblies on subsequent launches. Loader arguments do not fix missing macOS graphical-session access under SSH.

Under plain Wine, `scripts/launch_game.py` does not start `wine` directly: it detaches a small watcher process (`launch_game.py watch --log FILE -- wine …`) that runs the game, sends its stdout and stderr to `BepInEx/wine-latest.log`, and appends `wine: game process exited with status N` when the game exits — only the game's parent can reap it, and a negative `N` means it was killed by signal `N`. The previous launch's log rotates to `wine-latest.prev.log` the way Unity rotates `Player.log`. The game launches with `WINEDEBUG=-all,err+seh,warn+seh,err+module,trace+loaddll` so an unhandled exception prints its Wine backtrace, a missing DLL reports an `err:module` line, and the last `trace:loaddll` line shows how far module loading got before an early exit, while everything else stays silent. MoltenVK and DXVK need their own caps — `MVK_CONFIG_LOG_LEVEL=1` and `DXVK_LOG_LEVEL=error` — because they write to stderr outside Wine's debug channels, and MoltenVK's descriptor-pool warnings alone pour out hundreds of megabytes a minute at their default level. When the game dies during startup and `LogOutput.log` stops mid-boot, that file holds the reason the terminal never showed; compare it with the two Unity logs under `drive_c/users/crossover/AppData/LocalLow/Ved/Megabonk/` (`Player.log` for the failed boot, `Player-prev.log` for the healthy one before it). The watcher opens the log with a `watch: launched pid=… ppid=… session=… cwd=… tty=…` header, the full argv, and the environment variables whose names start with `WINE`, `DOTNET_`, `DYLD_`, `MVK_`, `DXVK_`, `MEGABONK_`, or `Steam`, so two launches can be compared after the shells that started them are gone.

`MEGABONK_RELAUNCH_DIAG=true` (environment or `.env`) enables extra exception diagnostics: `UnityCrashHandler64.exe` is renamed to `UnityCrashHandler64.exe.disabled` before the launch and `+seh` is appended to `WINEDEBUG` (through `MEGABONK_WINEDEBUG_EXTRA`), with Wine's `ShowCrashDialog` registry value set to 0. The next non-diagnostic relaunch restores the handler and drops the extra channel. Exit status 1 alone is not evidence of a native crash: Unity also deliberately exits that way after a failed window creation, without raising an unhandled exception.

An unknown command, malformed JSON, a missing or empty `path`, or a command that fails while running logs an error and moves the file to `rejected/` without replaying it.

The paths in `load-level` and `screenshot` are interpreted by the game process, so `run.py` converts them (see below).

### Launching and driving the game from SSH on macOS

For plain Wine, use the same commands from SSH as from the desktop:

```sh
task game:editor
uv run scripts/run.py state
uv run scripts/run.py screenshot /path/to/frame.png
uv run scripts/run.py quit
```

The same user must already be logged in to the Mac's graphical desktop. `scripts/launch_game.py` checks `Security.SessionGetInfo` for graphical access. A caller without it is handed off to a one-shot LaunchAgent in `gui/<uid>`; the watcher and Wine then run in the GUI session, while the existing file-based harness remains accessible over SSH. `task game:steam` uses the same handoff. No sudo, debugger wrapper, or desktop-terminal command is needed. This handoff does not cover the separate CrossOver launcher.

Without the handoff, Wine's macOS display driver can reject the SSH security session and unload before graphics initialization. Unity then exits with `Failed to create window`; MelonLoader can stop at `0 Plugins loaded` and `Player.log` can contain only physics initialization. Disabling the crash handler or redirecting stdin does not fix graphical-session access.

LaunchAgent definitions live under `~/Library/Application Support/Megabonk/launchd/`, not `~/Library/LaunchAgents/`, so nothing is configured to start at the next login. Jobs run once, without automatic restart. A stopped job is removed before reusing its prefix/target-specific label; a running or pending job is refused rather than killed. The resolved argv, Wine overrides and working directory are preserved. Only runtime environment settings (Wine, .NET, graphics, locale, path and the mod's diagnostic flags) are forwarded; unrelated SSH credentials and API tokens are not copied into the plist. Game output still goes to `BepInEx/wine-latest.log`; Steam's launch output goes beside its plist. A missing GUI domain or rejected bootstrap reports a launch error instead of silently falling back to a headless start.

Omit the old machine-local `.tools/exit-trace/wine` override and leave `MEGABONK_RELAUNCH_DIAG` unset or false for ordinary SSH launches. The Mac must remain logged in and awake; this is not a headless replacement for its desktop session.

### Music transport

The `music` command and the ungated console verbs `music.play [n]`, `music.pause`, `music.next`, `music.prev`, `music.track n`, `music.list` and `music.now` control the level's music. `music.play n` is an alias of `music.track n` (it sends the `track` action); the bare `music.play` resumes. `run.py music <action> [n]` and the MCP `music(action, track)` tool send the same payload: `{"command":"music","action":"play|pause|next|prev|track|list|now"}`, with `"track":<n>` for `track`.

Track numbers are 1-based, in playlist order, and `list`, `now` and the change message show each name without its leading file number (`37_The_Tourist` reads `The_Tourist`), because a pack's file numbers drift from playlist positions where a number is split (`06_01`, `06_02`); `tracks` in the result keeps the full clip names: clip-name order for a pack playlist (`bundle:<pack>/<clip>`), numeric filename order for runtime mp3s (`track:<name>`). `next` and `prev` wrap at both ends; `track` rejects a number outside 1 to the track count with an error. A track change goes through the same playback path as the boombox and the automatic advance, and the playlist remembers the chosen track, so the next loop advance or boombox press continues from it. The log records the new track as `Radio: playing ...`.

`play` and `pause` resume and pause the music source, so they also work on a stock stage. Each checks the source first and says what happened: `play` on a playing source replies `music is already playing`, and when resuming starts nothing it replies `no paused music to resume`; `pause` on a source that is not playing replies `music is not playing` and leaves it alone. `next`, `prev`, `track`, `list` and `now` reply `no custom playlist on this stage` when the level has no custom playlist (`playlist` is `false` in the result). Otherwise the result holds `message` (the text the console prints), `playlist`, `count`, `current` (1-based, 0 before a track is chosen) and `tracks`.

### Runtime music probe

With the harness enabled and a run loaded, copy an mp3 into the game's `UserData/CustomMusic/`, then use:

```sh
uv run scripts/run.py music-probe decode "01 Police Truck.mp3"
uv run scripts/run.py music-probe native-info
uv run scripts/run.py music-probe native-tone
uv run scripts/run.py music-probe native-play "01 Police Truck.mp3"
uv run scripts/run.py music-probe pitch --value 0.55
uv run scripts/run.py music-probe pitch
uv run scripts/run.py music-probe lowpass --value 400
uv run scripts/run.py music-probe lowpass
```

`native-info` resolves internal calls and reports their module/RVA and a short code prefix without creating a clip. `native-tone` creates a two-second, 440 Hz test clip; `native-play` decodes the mp3 into a real clip using the shared bounded decoder/resource owner. Both use the game's normal `MusicTrack` playback with no injected filter, and report clip length and maximum PCM roundtrip error across every uploaded block. Input is limited to 64 MiB compressed and 256 MiB decoded PCM per track; see [music.md](music.md#production-decoder-and-ownership). The native ABI is verified only on Win64 (including Wine); other platforms are rejected pending inspection. Native calls can crash the process if the ABI changes, so use a disposable test run. Relaunch to restore stock music after a native probe; `stop` and `detach` affect buffer injection only. Native resources are also released once another game track takes over.

For comparison, the older buffer-injection path is:

```sh
uv run scripts/run.py music-probe play "01 Police Truck.mp3"
uv run scripts/run.py music-probe status
uv run scripts/run.py music-probe detach
```

Paths are interpreted by the game; a file name resolves under `UserData/CustomMusic/`. Both playback paths decode the entire file synchronously. `play` loops PCM through a filter without resampling; `native-play` declares the clip's source rate so Unity handles sampling. This is a diagnostic, not a level music format. `decode` reports sample rate, channels, decoded samples, duration and peak; `status` reports callback counts, consumed frames, average copy time, source controls and probe errors. After `native-play`, its `decodeMs` includes native creation/readback as well as decoding. Its `playing` and `attached` fields describe buffer injection only, not native clip playback; inspect `sourceIsPlaying`, `sourceClip` and `stockTrack` for that. A failure can be reported as `error` inside the returned probe object; inspect it as well as the harness envelope.

Actions are `decode`, `native-info`, `native-tone`, `native-play`, `play`, `stop`, `status`, `restart`, `duck`, `unduck`, `pause`, `unpause`, `mute`, `unmute`, `clip-null`, `pitch`, `lowpass`, `detach`, `radio-next`, `seek`, `menu-music` and `stage-music`. `duck` defaults to 0.2; `--value` overrides it, sets source pitch, or sets source low-pass cutoff in Hz. Pitch and cutoff with an explicit value are held by a postfix after `MusicController.Update`, because the game otherwise resets them before a listener can compare. `pitch` without a value releases the hold and sets 1; `lowpass` without a value releases the hold and sets 22,000 Hz, then leaves subsequent changes to the game. Confirm `heldPitch`/`heldCutoff` and a later property reading when testing. `unduck` sets both duck multipliers to 1. `stop` stops overwriting samples; `detach` also removes the injected component and releases held controls. Neither restores a clip removed with `clip-null` nor resets mute; relaunch for a clean stock baseline after destructive comparisons. `restart` restarts the source, but is not required when attaching to a playing source.

For integration checks, `radio-next` invokes the scene boombox's real `Interact` method, `seek --value <seconds>` moves within the current clip (use near its end to exercise progression), and `stage-music` calls the normal stage playback path. `menu-music` calls the music controller's menu handler, exercising session cleanup without navigating the UI; it does not leave the run itself. `status` includes `levelMusicReference` and `runtimeSession`. Relaunch with a level containing a `track:` reference to test production startup discovery/selection, rather than using `native-play`, which bypasses them.

The default `--variant il2cpp` uses the working `Il2CppStructArray<float>` signature. `managed` reproduces the unsupported `float[]` callback, which Unity never dispatches. Detach before comparing variants. Frozen editor gameplay is not an audio pause and does not prevent the game resetting audio properties; use the held controls for comparisons. Duck multiplier propagation through `MusicController.Update` was observed with resumed gameplay. See [music.md](music.md#runtime-pcm-probe) for the audible results and limitations.

### Results

Before moving a command file to `done/` or `rejected/`, the mod writes `harness/results/<command file name>`: UTF-8 JSON, one object, written to `<name>.tmp` and renamed so a reader never sees a partial file. A command that succeeds writes `{"command":"<wire name>","ok":true,"result":<object or null>}`. A command that fails while running writes `{"command":"<wire name>","ok":false,"error":"<message>"}`. A file that does not parse writes the same failure shape with `"command":null`. Every command other than `state`, `ui-tree`, `click-widget`, `save-level`, `new-level`, `list-levels`, `palette`, `query-objects`, `teleport`, `grind-slide`, `dismiss-levelup`, `dismiss-offers`, `interact`, `music`, `music-probe` and `steam-ready` has `"result":null`. The result file is written before the command file moves, so a caller that sees the file in `done/` or `rejected/` can read the result at once. Result files are never deleted by the mod.

`run.py` reads them with `read_result(harness, name)`, which returns `None` when the file is absent or not valid JSON. Every subcommand that sends a command exits 1 and prints the `error` text on stderr when the result has `ok:false`, even if the command file landed in `done/`. A missing result file does not fail a command, so `run.py` still works against a mod built before results existed.

### Steamworks readiness

`steam-ready` succeeds with `{"ready":true,"buildId":"<id>"}` once the game's Steamworks client answers `GetAppBuildId`, and with `{"ready":false,"reason":"<why>"}` while no client is available; `reason` is `steam-not-running` when `SteamAPI.IsSteamRunning()` positively says Steam is down and `client-not-initialized` otherwise, including when that call cannot be consulted either. A build id of `0` counts as no build id: a client with no depot identity answers that way and it must not be stamped onto a level. Not-ready answers also carry what they were read against — `steamRunning`, `steamSession`, `steamLibrary` and `failInitEnv` — since the same verdict means different things under the real shim and under the development stub. The not-initialized fault is an expected answer during startup, so `SteamReadiness.Probe` catches that one message (`Steamworks is not initialized`) and returns it as data instead of letting it reach the harness's generic error handler, which would log an ERROR with an IL2CPP stack trace on every poll. Any other fault still propagates and is logged. `run.py steam-ready` prints the verdict (`ready (build <id>)` / `not ready (<reason>)`), appends the evidence as `[steam=…, session=…, library=…, failInitEnv=…]` when the mod reports it, and exits 1 on `ready:false`. `run.py relaunch` polls the same verdict: it proceeds on `ready:true`, continues after a 15-second grace once a definite `ready:false` has stood (the offline case, where the client will never become ready), fails with `relaunch: Steamworks did not become ready` only if the game never answers at all, and ends at once with `relaunch: game exited during startup` if the game process dies while polling. A mod that returns no result counts as ready. A long-running caller such as the MCP server has to be restarted to pick up a changed `run.py`; an old one treats any `ok:true` as ready.

### State

`run.py state` prints the `result` object of the `state` command as one line of JSON. A field the mod cannot source is `null`.

| Field | Type | Source and when it is `null` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `levelLoaded` | bool | `CustomLevelState.IsActive`: the run was started through the mod's level loader |
| `killFloorArmed` | bool | The kill-floor watcher armed for the current level |
| `editor` | bool | Editor mode is active |
| `frozen` | bool | `MyTime.paused` |
| `invulnerable` | bool | The harness's invulnerability is active on the current player's inventory |
| `spawnsOff` | bool | The harness has stopped enemy spawns on the current `EnemyManager` |
| `coordinatesEnabled` | bool | Explicit coordinate preference, off at startup and unchanged by editor/HUD toggles or level loads |
| `coordinatesVisible` | bool | The coordinate label exists and its HUD is visible; can be false while enabled during loading, after death, with the HUD hidden or outside a supported custom run |
| `cursorVisible` | bool | `Cursor.visible` at the time of the call |
| `cursorLocked` | bool | `Cursor.lockState` is not `None`; both cursor fields read the game's actual cursor, which the console and editor palette unlock while open |
| `player` | object or null | `{"position":[x,y,z],"cell":[column,row]}`. `null` when there is no player in the scene (menu, loading). `cell` is `null` when the position label's grid is not armed (no kill floor), and can lie outside the grid |
| `editorTool` | string or null | The active tool name (`Raise`, `Lower`, `Slope`, `Remove`, `Place`, `Select`). `null` outside editor mode, where the tool is not read |
| `selected` | string or null | The selected object as `gen:<genId>` or `placement:<id>`. `null` outside editor mode or when nothing is selected |
| `paletteEntries` | int or null | The number of entries in the catalog palette. `null` outside editor mode, where the palette is not read |
| `edits` | object or null | `{"count":n,"dirty":bool,"lastSavedName":s,"saveStatus":s}`. `count` is the number of edits on the stack, `dirty` is whether that differs from the last save or load, `lastSavedName` is the file name of the last save or load (`null` until one happens) and `saveStatus` is the outcome of the last save attempt: `none`, `saved` or `failed`. `null` outside editor mode |
| `screen` | `[width, height]` | `Screen.width` and `Screen.height` in pixels; screen coordinates for input use this space |

## MCP server

`megabonk_harness.mcp_server` (`src/megabonk_harness/mcp_server.py`) exposes the harness as an MCP server named `megabonk`, so an agent can call typed tools instead of shelling out to `run.py`, and gets screenshots back as inline `image/png` content. It ships as the `megabonk-mcp` command of the `megabonk_harness` package, which also holds `run.py`'s helpers (`megabonk-harness` is the same CLI as `scripts/run.py`), and resolves the game directory the same way `run.py` does. The files under `scripts/` that carry the old names (`run.py`, `game_paths.py`, `launch_game.py`, `mcp_server.py`) are shims over the package; the dependency list lives only in `pyproject.toml`.

stdout is the MCP protocol channel, so the server writes nothing to stdout; everything reaches the client as tool results. Blocking waits (sending a command, waiting for the game to exit, the whole relaunch sequence) run off the event loop so one tool call never stalls the server.

Tools: `relaunch(level?, map?, stage?, tier?, seed?, unseeded?)` (reports progress through the MCP context and returns the final `state`), `load(level, map?, stage?, tier?, seed?, unseeded?)`, `quit()`, `screenshot(directory?, filename?, overwrite?, scale?, crop?)` (returns the PNG inline), `state()`, `editor(on)`, `freeze()`, `resume()`, `invulnerable(on)`, `kill()`, `restart_run()`, `spawns(on)`, `camera(position?, rotation?, clear?)`, `log_tail(pattern?, since_launch?, max_lines?)`, `log_wait(pattern, timeout?, since_launch?)`, `paths()`, and the input and gameplay tools `key(key, modifiers?, action?)`, `mouse(action, x?, y?, button?, delta?)`, `ui_tree(x?, y?)`, `click_widget(name)`, `save_level(level?)`, `new_level(level?, map?, stage?, tier?, size?)`, `list_levels()`, `palette()`, `query_objects(type, fields?, jq?)`, `type_text(text)`, `teleport(position?, cell?)`, `item(name, amount?)`, `grind_slide(held?)`, `dismiss_levelup(choice?)`, `dismiss_offers(choice?, skip?)` and `interact(type, max_distance?, force?)` (`type` is required; an empty or missing type is a tool error and nothing reaches the game). A command the mod rejects, a timeout, or a result with `ok:false` surfaces as a tool error carrying the result file's `error` text (or `<command> <outcome>` when there is no result file).

`log_tail` reads `BepInEx/LogOutput.log`: `since_launch` (default true) trims to the last `DevHarness: enabled` line, `pattern` keeps lines matching a regex (an invalid regex is a tool error carrying the parse failure), and `max_lines` (default 50) caps the result to the last N matching lines. `log_wait` polls the same log until a line matches the regex `pattern`, returns that line, and is a tool error after `timeout` seconds (default 30) or for an invalid regex; `since_launch` (default true) ignores lines before the last `DevHarness: enabled`. Use it after `load` or `relaunch` instead of sleeping, for example `log_wait("KillFloor: armed")`. `paths()` returns `{game_dir, harness, latest_log, levels_dir}`, where `latest_log` is `<game_dir>/BepInEx/LogOutput.log` and `levels_dir` is `<game_dir>/UserData/Levels`, the folder the editor saves custom levels to.

The input tools validate their arguments before sending anything and raise a tool error for a bad combination: `teleport` needs exactly one of `position` (three numbers) or `cell` (two integers), `mouse` `move` needs both `x` and `y` and the other actions take both or neither, `scroll` needs `delta` and no other action accepts it, `key` accepts `modifiers` only with `action` `tap`, and `key` checks the key and modifier names against the mod's list (a mirror of `key_names.cs`, kept in step by a test) so an unknown name fails with the valid names before anything is sent. `ui_tree` takes both or neither of `x` and `y`. Each returns the result file's object (`ui_tree`, `click_widget`, `save_level`, `list_levels`, `teleport`, `grind_slide`, `dismiss_levelup`, `dismiss_offers` and `interact` carry data; the others return `null`). Coordinates are screenshot pixels with the origin at the top left, as in "Key, mouse and type commands", which also lists the valid key names. Input is queued over the next frames, so a `screenshot` straight after may predate its effect.

### Running console lines

`console(line)` sends one raw console line through the overlay's registry, gating and in-process dispatch without opening the panel or injecting keys. For example, `console(line="help player")` or `uv run scripts/run.py console 'help player'`. Quote the entire line as one CLI argument; double quotes inside it keep console arguments with spaces together (`run.py console 'level.load "Bramblecrown Hill"'`). Missing, empty or whitespace-only lines are rejected.

The harness reply is `{"command":"console","ok":true,"result":{"ok":true,"lines":["..."]}}`. `result.lines` contains the same printed lines as the overlay, including command echoes where applicable, split on newlines before visual wrapping. `result.ok` is false for a registry refusal, bad arguments or a failed dispatched command; the outer `ok` describes whether the harness handled the console request. MCP returns this envelope even for a console refusal, preserving the reply. `run.py console` prints the lines and exits 1 for a console refusal or failed command. Gated verbs reply `requires MEGABONK_DEV` when the game was launched without the dev flag. Output goes into the same scrollback and accepted lines into the same history as typed input, even before the panel is first opened; `clear` and `history` work normally. Restart a running MCP server to discover the added tool.

### Clearing the console log

The in-game console replays its persisted output after a relaunch (see "Persistence" in the console section), so a console line recorded before a behavior or output change still shows the old text. `clear_console_log()` (`run.py clear-console-log`) deletes `<game>/UserData/console_log.jsonl`, so the next launch starts with an empty history and scrollback; it returns the path and whether a log was removed, and a missing log is not an error. It works on the file only: a running game keeps its in-memory history until it relaunches, so clear before `relaunch()`.

### Retaining screenshots

`screenshot(directory=None, filename=None)` returns two content blocks: the inline PNG and text reporting its saved absolute path. With no arguments it saves `megabonk-<timestamp-in-nanoseconds>.png` in the repository's gitignored `.tools/evidence`, so captures never land in the working tree. Optional keyword arguments select the destination:

```python
screenshot()
screenshot(directory=".tools/evidence")
screenshot(directory=".tools/evidence", filename="final-editor-slope.png")
screenshot(directory="/path/to/screenshots", filename="forest.png")
```

Relative directories resolve against the server's working directory; absolute directories are supported. Missing directories, including parents, are created automatically. `filename` must be a single basename ending in `.png`, not a path; path separators and invalid filename characters are rejected. Omit it for a timestamped name in the chosen directory.

Existing files, directories and symlinks are not overwritten by default: a collision returns a tool error asking for another filename or directory, including if the destination appears during capture. `overwrite=True` replaces an existing file instead (the capture is moved over it atomically; a directory at the destination is still an error). The server captures into a temporary subdirectory, validates the PNG, and publishes it using an exclusive hard link on the same filesystem; the filesystem must support hard links. Temporary captures are cleaned up on success or failure, though newly created destination directories remain. Invalid destinations, permissions and capture failures return actionable tool errors rather than a saved path. `crop=[x, y, width, height]` (screenshot pixels, applied first) and `scale` (greater than 0, at most 1) shrink the inline image to cut token cost; the saved file is always the full capture. A crop outside the capture or a bad `scale` is a tool error. A server restart is needed to pick up changes to its tool signatures.

To verify retention through MCP, call `screenshot(filename="editor-check.png")`, inspect the inline image and open the reported path as a PNG. A second call with the same arguments must return a collision error and leave the first PNG unchanged.

### End-to-end check

A walkthrough that exercises the whole chain (agent, MCP, harness files, mod, game) using only the `megabonk` tools, in editor mode on the example forest level:

1. `relaunch()` quits any running game, starts a fresh one, loads `levels/example-forest.mblevel.json` and enters editor mode. The returned state has `editor: true`.
2. `screenshot()` and look for the catalog palette on the left.
3. `click_widget(name="Palette:<catalog id>")`, or `mouse(action="click", x, y)` on a palette row using pixel coordinates read from the screenshot, selects that entry and switches to the Place tool; a later `screenshot()` shows it selected in the palette title and `state()` reports `editorTool`.
4. A `mouse` click on a tile places the entry at the cursor. Before that click, `key(key="R")`, `F` and `G` turn the ghost preview in 90 degree steps (yaw, pitch, roll; pass `action="tap"` with `modifiers=["LeftShift"]` to reverse), and the click writes exactly the rotation shown. `key(key="Alpha6")` and a click on the object select it, where `key(key="R")` rotates the selected object. `type_text("e2e")` after a click on the level-name field types into it.
5. `teleport(cell=[cx, cz])` to a distant cell, wait about a second, then `state()` to confirm `player` and `cell` moved and `editor` is still true, and `screenshot()`. In editor mode the free-fly camera does not follow the player, so the check is the `state` position and the on-screen `me: pos(...)` label, not the view. The example forest grid is 15x15, so cells run 0 to 14; a cell outside it comes back as a tool error.
6. `quit()`.

If a step fails, `log_tail(pattern="Harness|Error")` shows whether the mod saw the command. A rejected command carries the mod's error text in the tool error, which says which layer refused it.

Requirements: uv 0.11.32 or newer in the 0.11 series (the version pinned in `.tool-versions`; the package builds with `uv_build>=0.11.32,<0.12`) and mise, verified with 2026.10.15, to install the rest of the pinned toolchain with `mise install`. Clone the repository once and register the server with `uvx`, which builds the `megabonk_harness` package from the repository on first use; nothing is installed by hand and the entry carries no machine-specific path (the repository's `.mcp.json` ships the same entry next to unrelated servers; a machine that only drives the game needs just this):

```json
{
  "mcpServers": {
    "megabonk": {
      "command": "uvx",
      "args": ["--from", "git+https://github.com/pythoninthegrass/megabonk_mods", "megabonk-mcp"],
      "env": { "MEGABONK_REPO": "${MEGABONK_REPO:-}" }
    }
  }
}
```

A checkout is mandatory because the server reads `.env`, `levels/`, `.tools/evidence` and `taskfile.yml` from it, while the server code itself runs from uv's cache. It finds the checkout from `MEGABONK_REPO`, then from the working directory's ancestors, then from the tree the code was loaded from (only a source-tree run, such as `uv run megabonk-mcp` in the clone or an editable install, ever reaches that step). The entry passes `MEGABONK_REPO` through from the client's environment: a client started inside the clone needs nothing, and any other client needs `export MEGABONK_REPO=/path/to/megabonk_mods` in the shell profile that starts it. The `${MEGABONK_REPO:-}` form expands to an empty string when the variable is unset, which falls through to the working-directory search; opencode's equivalent is `{env:MEGABONK_REPO}`. With no checkout found the command exits with an error naming `MEGABONK_REPO` instead of reading `.env` or writing evidence from the cache. `task` runs (the relaunch) use the checkout as their working directory.

`uvx` resolves dependencies fresh from the package metadata (the checkout's `uv.lock` and `exclude-newer` do not apply to a git install) and caches the built commit, so after pulling a change to `src/megabonk_harness` run `uv cache clean megabonk-mods` or add `--refresh` to the entry's arguments. For development in the clone, `uv run megabonk-mcp` or `uv tool install --editable .` run the live tree instead.

The server resolves the game directory from the environment and the nearest `.env` the same way `run.py` does, so the entry's `env` block only needs `MEGABONK_REPO` (above) unless a setting such as `MEGABONK_MCP_ENCODING` is wanted (see [Result encoding](#result-encoding)). Confirm it with `/mcp`, which lists `megabonk` as connected.

### Result encoding

Structured tool results (every tool that returns the harness result, plus `paths`) pass through one function, `_encode` in `megabonk_harness.mcp_server`. The `MEGABONK_MCP_ENCODING` environment variable, read through python-decouple (process environment, then `.env`; the `megabonk` entry of `.mcp.json` can also set it), selects the format:

| Value | Text content | `structuredContent` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `json` | indented JSON | present, identical to the payload |
| `json-min` (default, also when unset) | JSON without whitespace | omitted |
| `toon` | [TOON](https://github.com/toon-format/toon) | omitted |

`json-min` and `toon` omit `structuredContent` because a client that forwards it would send the payload twice and cancel the saving. An unknown value is a tool error raised before the command is sent, so a bad setting does not follow a game action. Errors, screenshots (image content), `log_tail`, `log_wait` and `quit` are never re-encoded.

`toon-format` is pinned to `1.2.0` in the script header and in the `scripts:test` command. An unpinned install resolves to `0.1.0`, a stub whose `encode()` raises `NotImplementedError`; `test_toon_encode_is_not_the_stub_release` fails on it.

The default was chosen by a rule fixed before measuring: switch to TOON only if its total token count is at least 15% below minified JSON across the captured `state`, `list_levels` and `ui_tree` payloads, and no single payload is more than 5% worse than minified JSON. The payloads are in `tests/fixtures/mcp_payloads/`, captured from a live editor session on the example forest level. Tokens were counted with tiktoken `cl100k_base`, a proxy for Claude's tokenizer, so treat the percentages as approximate.

| Payload | JSON (indented) | JSON (minified) | TOON | TOON vs minified |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `state` | 176 | 100 | 122 | 22.0% worse |
| `list_levels` | 95 | 69 | 71 | 2.9% worse |
| `ui_tree` (54 widgets) | 3128 | 1979 | 1375 | 30.5% better |
| Total | 3399 | 2148 | 1568 | 27.0% better |

TOON wins on the uniform array of widgets and loses on the small nested `state` object, so the total passes the 15% bar and the per-payload bar fails. The default is therefore `json-min`, which was 37% below indented JSON in total on the same payloads (2148 tokens against 3399) and does worse than TOON only on `ui_tree`. Set `MEGABONK_MCP_ENCODING=toon` for sessions that read many `ui_tree` results, or `json` to get indented text with `structuredContent` back. `json-min` was checked live through Claude Code: `state`, `list_levels` and `ui_tree` return parseable compact JSON, errors stay plain text and `screenshot` still returns an image. The uniform-rows case from the live-object query was not measured because that query does not exist yet; re-measure once it lands, since its rows are the shape TOON suits best.

## Remote Steam hosts and gamepadui

Standing up a gamescope + Steam gamepadui session, forcing the Proton baseline
and measuring the real prefix, installing and verifying the player package,
transferring THPS content, and driving stock menus over SSH with `run.py menu`
are documented in [gamepadui.md](gamepadui.md). The player package needs Python
3.11 or newer on the target, but no repository checkout, uv, .NET SDK or separate
Wine installation.

### GNOME screenshots over SSH

GNOME exposes screenshot capture through D-Bus. Direct calls to
`org.gnome.Shell.Screenshot` can return `AccessDenied` over SSH; use the session
bus's `org.freedesktop.portal.Desktop` service at
`/org/freedesktop/portal/desktop`, calling
`org.freedesktop.portal.Screenshot.Screenshot` with an empty parent window and
`interactive=false`. Subscribe to `org.freedesktop.portal.Request.Response`
before calling: the method returns a request handle, while the response supplies
the saved PNG's `uri` (on success, response code 0). This worked over SSH in the
logged-in GNOME session, saving under `~/Pictures`; retrieve that file with
`scp`. Unlike a nested Xwayland capture, it showed the game's warning overlay.

### Reaching the X server from an SSH shell

A shell outside the desktop session has neither `DISPLAY` nor `XAUTHORITY`, so Steam and the game cannot open a window. Steam reports "Unable to open X11 display" in the shell, or shows a zenity "Unable to open a connection to X" dialog on the desktop that nobody sees over SSH. The name of the cookie file differs by desktop: KDE Wayland writes `/run/user/<uid>/xauth_*`, GNOME's mutter writes `/run/user/<uid>/.mutter-Xwaylandauth.<random>`, and the random part changes on every login.

`task game:run` sets `DISPLAY=:0` and, when `XAUTHORITY` is unset, reads the `-auth` argument from the running Xwayland command line, falling back to the first `/run/user/<uid>/xauth_*` (the KDE name). That covers KDE and GNOME. `run.py relaunch` launches through `task game:run`, so it needs nothing extra. `run.py menu` reads `DISPLAY` and `XAUTHORITY` from its own environment, so export them first, as does any launch that bypasses `task game:run`. The same lookup by hand:

```sh
export DISPLAY=:0
export XAUTHORITY="$(pgrep -ax Xwayland | sed -n 's/.*-auth \([^ ]*\).*/\1/p' | head -n1)"
```

A failed launch leaves a Steam client and its error dialog behind. Clear them with `pkill -x zenity` and `pkill -x steam` (exact names, never `pkill -f`) before retrying. Verified on KDE Wayland and GNOME Wayland. A plain Xorg session and other compositors are unconfirmed: check `pgrep -af 'Xorg|Xwayland'` for the `-auth` argument there.

### Verified menu regression under Proton

Verified on 2026-10-31 under Proton Experimental with Megabonk 1.0.69 and MelonLoader 0.7.3 (see [gamepadui.md](gamepadui.md) for the host setup), the installed Windows THPS pack, and Calcium selected. Input came through Linux uinput mouse clicks (with X11 absolute pointer positioning) and a virtual Xbox 360 controller into the stock menus; the harness supplied screenshots, read-only object queries and quit between DLL builds. No save reset, developer character override or `load-level` command was used.

1. On Forest, select Tier 3, then select Warehouse. Only Tier 1 remains visible, selected and selectable; hidden tiers are unselected and cannot be selected. Click Tier 1 and Confirm with the mouse: Warehouse renders and the pause panel identifies `Warehouse / Tier 1`.
2. Using controller D-pad/A, select Forest Tier 3, move left to the map list, down to Warehouse and select it. Focus lands on visible Tier 1. Down goes directly to Challenges and Up returns to Tier 1. Selecting Tier 1 again moves focus to Confirm; A starts Warehouse. The final launch logged `LevelLoader: starting map=Forest stage=0 tier=0 seed=20261007`, then `KillFloor: armed`, and the rendered pause panel identified Warehouse.
3. Switch from Warehouse to a native map before starting. Forest again offers its three unlocked tiers, and controller navigation can select Tier 3. Graveyard retains its native single-tier layout. Selecting a different native map can leave focus in the map list; select the same entry again to move into its tiers, as in the unmodified menu.

Regression unit tests cover a locked first tier with a stale higher-tier selection, navigation repair on refresh without premature selection, and repair after native visibility has been restored. Keep the group indexes stable during the native handler; hiding buttons alone reproduces the stale hidden-tier bug.

## Launching under Wine (macOS)

Plain Wine is the default macOS runtime. It runs against the Wine prefix named by `MEGABONK_WINE_PREFIX` (default `~/.local/share/megabonk/wine`), which has to hold a Windows Steam install with Megabonk; the repo does not create or populate a prefix. To reuse the bottle CrossOver created, set `MEGABONK_WINE_PREFIX=~/Library/Application Support/CrossOver/Bottles/Steam` in `.env`, and its saves, Steam login and installed game carry over. The setup was verified with Wine 11.18 and DXVK-macOS on Apple silicon with Rosetta, using that bottle as the prefix.

```sh
task wine:install       # pinned Wine build and DXVK DLLs; skips what is already installed
task game:steam         # Steam client, detached; wait about 30 seconds for logon
task game:melonloader:install
task game:run           # Megabonk.exe, detached
```

`task wine:install` runs `wine:app` and `wine:dxvk` from `taskfiles/wine.yml`. The pins (version, URL, sha256 for each) are defined once in that file's `vars` and can be overridden by exporting `WINE_VERSION`, `WINE_URL`, `WINE_SHA256`, `DXVK_VERSION`, `DXVK_URL` or `DXVK_SHA256`, or by setting them in `.env`, which the root `taskfile.yml` loads with `dotenv` for every task. Each step downloads its archive to a temporary directory and checks the sha256 before extracting or copying anything, so a mismatch stops the task with nothing changed. An unset or empty override falls back to the pin. `wine:dxvk` installs into the prefix named by `MEGABONK_WINE_PREFIX` and needs it to exist (the `drive_c/windows/system32` directory); on a machine without one it prints `wine:dxvk skipped: no Wine prefix at <path>` and exits 0, leaving the Wine build installed, so run `task wine:dxvk` again once the prefix exists. The repo does not create a prefix. The skip is Task's command-level `if:`, which needs Task 3.47 or newer (pinned in `.tool-versions`); an older Task ignores `if:` without a warning and the step then fails on the missing prefix.

- **Wine build.** The Gcenx `wine-devel` 11.18 build is extracted to `~/Applications/wine11/Wine Devel.app`, then the quarantine attribute is removed and the app is ad-hoc signed so Gatekeeper lets it run. Set `MEGABONK_WINE` to use a different binary. Wine 7.7 (the Game Porting Toolkit cask) crashes Steam with an unhandled page fault, and Homebrew's `wine-stable` is disabled.
- **DXVK.** Wine's own Direct3D path reports no supported feature level on macOS, and Unity fails with `Failed to initialize player`. The task copies the x64 `d3d11.dll` and `d3d10core.dll` from DXVK-macOS (async build, `v1.10.3-20230507-repack`) into the prefix's `drive_c/windows/system32`. The first run saves the DLLs it replaces in `drive_c/windows/dxvk-orig/` and never overwrites that backup. `drive_c/windows/dxvk.installed` records the installed pin. MoltenVK ships inside the Wine build. The DXVK release publishes no checksum for its archive, so the pinned sha256 was computed locally from the downloaded file and is not independently verified.
- **`USER=crossover`.** Wine takes the Windows profile name from the macOS user. Without the override Wine creates `C:\users\<macOS user>` with no saves, and the game starts from an empty profile. CrossOver ran as `crossover`, so the saves live in `drive_c/users/crossover`. `scripts/launch_game.py` sets `USER` and `LOGNAME` to `crossover` for every Wine call it makes.
- **`-silent`.** The Steam UI renders as a black window under Wine because its embedded browser does not draw. `-silent` keeps the main window closed while the client still logs on and serves Steamworks. The client must be logged on before the game starts, or Steamworks fails to initialize. It cannot show a login prompt, so sign in through the macOS Steam client or CrossOver if the session expires.
- **`winhttp.dll`.** BepInEx's doorstop needs `winhttp=native,builtin`. The plain-Wine game launch passes `winhttp=n,b;d3d11,d3d10core=n,b` in `WINEDLLOVERRIDES`, along with the native DXVK DLLs. The parked MelonLoader `version.dll` and its prefix override remain available for rollback, but must not be active alongside the doorstop. No MelonLoader command-line switches are passed to BepInEx.
- **Detaching.** `scripts/launch_game.py` starts Wine with `subprocess.Popen(..., start_new_session=True)`, so the game and Steam survive the task runner and a closed terminal. A trailing `&` does not work: Task's shell kills its background children when the task ends.

Shut Steam down with `wine 'C:\Program Files (x86)\Steam\steam.exe' -shutdown` (same `WINEPREFIX` and `USER`), never while the game is running, because that can break the Steamworks session and Cloud sync.

### Setting up a second machine

The repo does not create a prefix, and Steam cannot show a login prompt under Wine, so a second Mac gets its prefix by copying one that already works. Copy the prefix directory (the one that contains `drive_c`) to `~/.local/share/megabonk/wine` on the other machine, skipping what is volatile or not needed:

```sh
rsync -ah --stats \
  --exclude='/drive_c/Program Files (x86)/Steam/steamapps/common/<other game>/' \
  --exclude='/drive_c/Program Files (x86)/Steam/steamapps/appmanifest_<other app id>.acf' \
  --exclude='/drive_c/Program Files (x86)/Steam/logs/' --exclude='/drive_c/Program Files (x86)/Steam/dumps/' \
  --exclude='/drive_c/users/crossover/AppData/Local/Steam/htmlcache/' \
  "<source prefix>/" <host>:.local/share/megabonk/wine/
```

With only Megabonk kept, a prefix of about 12 GB copies as about 4.5 GB. The copy carries the Steam login, saves, installed loaders, DLL overrides and DXVK DLLs. The original second-machine check used MelonLoader; for the current plugin, activate BepInEx as described below before running `task game:steam` followed by `uv run scripts/run.py relaunch`. Steam allows one session per account: starting Steam from the copy signs the account out everywhere else, including the other machine's Steam and a native macOS Steam client. Copy while Steam is stopped when possible; a copy taken while it runs still booted, but the registry files can change underneath the copy. Check the result with `uv run scripts/game_paths.py` (it should print the new prefix) and `BepInEx/LogOutput.log` (`DevHarness: enabled`, `KillFloor: armed` and `EditorMode: entered`).

This was verified on a second Apple silicon Mac (Wine 11.18, DXVK-macOS async, Rosetta): the game launched with MelonLoader, `relaunch` loaded the example Forest level into editor mode, and `screenshot` returned the editor overlay. The mod builds there with no extra setup, because the build reads the same prefix setting and default; see [modding-setup.md](modding-setup.md).

## Running BepInEx under Wine

The **Megabonk Level Editor** Thunderstore package, clean-profile verification and
release/rollback procedure are documented in [Thunderstore packaging](thunderstore.md).

BepInEx 6 (`BepInExPack_IL2CPP` 6.0.0-be.788) boots Megabonk under the same plain-Wine setup as MelonLoader: the repo launch path (`task game:run`, Wine 11.18, DXVK) runs `Megabonk.exe` with the doorstop loaded and the chainloader starts. Verified with a published community plugin loading (`Loading [...]`, `Loaded <Plugin>`, `Chainloader startup complete` in `BepInEx/LogOutput.log`, empty `ErrorLog.log`). The first start needs network: BepInEx downloads unity libs from `unity.bepinex.dev` and runs Cpp2IL and interop generation, about 20 seconds.

BepInEx and MelonLoader cannot be active together. MelonLoader's `version.dll` proxy in the game directory blocks the doorstop's startup, so a BepInEx install replaces MelonLoader: `version.dll` must be removed or renamed out of the game directory (rename it back to go the other way). `winhttp.dll` (the doorstop proxy) and `doorstop_config.ini` then sit in the game directory beside `Megabonk.exe`, with `BepInEx/` and `dotnet/` under it.

The proxy is the switch, not the folders. Wine resolves `version` native-first with no help from the registry, so moving `MelonLoader/`, `Mods/` and `UserLibs/` aside while `version.dll` stays in place still starts MelonLoader — and the bootstrap then recreates a stub `MelonLoader/` holding only `Latest.log` and `Logs/`, because its own `net6/` went with the moved folder:

```text
    [ERROR] [MelonLoader.Bootstrap] Runtime config not found at: '<game dir>\MelonLoader\net6\MelonLoader.runtimeconfig.json'
```

That line prints to the console during the launch, where it is easy to mistake for a failed file move in the step before it. A `MelonLoader/` directory that reappears after being moved, or that line in `MelonLoader/Latest.log`, means the proxy is still active: check for `version.dll` in the game directory rather than for the folders, and delete the recreated stub once the proxy is parked.

Three things a mod manager's Apply leaves wrong, all fatal and silent (doorstop's `winhttp.dll` loads, but no `BepInEx/LogOutput.log` ever appears):

- **The pack's `dotnet/` folder is not written.** BepInEx needs `dotnet/` (187 files, including `coreclr.dll`), `.doorstop_version` and `changelog.txt` beside `doorstop_config.ini`, which points at `dotnet\coreclr.dll`. A package or installer must copy them from the pack zip; a manager Apply alone leaves them out.
- **The `winhttp` DLL override is not created.** Wine resolves `winhttp` to its builtin before the game directory's proxy, so the prefix needs `"winhttp"="native,builtin"` under `[Software\\Wine\\DllOverrides]`. `wine reg add` fails in a headless/SSH session, so the working edit is a line in the prefix's `user.reg` with no Wine process running (Wine rewrites the file while alive). A Steam launch under Proton can carry the same override as its launch option, `WINEDLLOVERRIDES="winhttp=n,b" %command%`, with no registry edit at all; it shows up in the launched command line ahead of `proton waitforexitandrun`, which is how to confirm the game actually got it. An app with no launch option and no registry line fails the same silent way.
- **`doorstop_config.ini` can name a path outside the game folder.** A manager may record a host path for `target_assembly` and `dll_search_path_override`, sometimes without a Windows drive letter, and doorstop then loads no BepInEx core. Both values belong relative to the game folder; `task wine:bepinex:repair` normalizes them. See [Repairing an r2modmac Apply](#repairing-an-r2modmac-apply).

A mod manager's own Play does not work for the Windows build under Wine: it routes through the CrossOver runner and the bottle's `steam.exe` and never plain Wine, and the game never comes up that way. CrossOver's launcher gates its runner on its license, so an expired trial puts up its dialog instead of starting anything — the bottle's `[Software\\CodeWeavers\\CrossOver\\cxoffice]` registry entries (`NagTime` and friends) only record that state, they do not control it. Launch through `task game:run` (or Steam) instead; the manager is for file management only.

### Repairing an r2modmac Apply

From a source checkout on the Mac holding the game, quit Megabonk, install the
BepInEx pack and editor in r2modmac, and Apply the profile. Then run:

```sh
task wine:bepinex:repair -- --download --dry-run
task wine:bepinex:repair -- --download
task game:run
```

The repair uses `scripts/game_paths.py` to resolve the game folder, including
`MEGABONK_GAME_DIR` and `MEGABONK_WINE_PREFIX` overrides. To select another folder,
pass `--game-dir "/path/to/Megabonk"` after `--`. Paths inside CrossOver bottles
are supported too; this does not change which Wine runtime launches the game.

`scripts/repair_bepinex.py` restores only missing `dotnet/` files,
`.doorstop_version` and `changelog.txt` from BepInExPack IL2CPP **6.0.788**.
It verifies the archive's pinned SHA-256 even when reusing the cache at
`.tools/vendor-cache/`. Downloads require `--download`; to use a ZIP already
downloaded from Thunderstore, pass `--pack-zip "/path/to/pack.zip"` instead.
`--dry-run` validates and lists changes without writing files, including the cache.
Normal runs print one result line, distinguishing a completed repair from an
already-repaired install, and append the file list and launch guidance
to `.tools/logs/bepinex-repair.log`. Failures remain visible on stderr and are
also logged when the log is writable. Dry-run prints its preview without logging.

An active MelonLoader `version.dll` is parked as `version.dll.bak`. The helper
refuses an existing backup, an unknown proxy without a `MelonLoader/` folder,
symlink destinations, a running Megabonk process, missing manager-installed
BepInEx files, or existing runtime files that differ from the pinned pack.
Resolve a refusal rather than deleting a backup or replacing another runtime.
Repeated runs leave matching files alone. Plugins, configuration, other
MelonLoader files, and `UserData/` are not changed. Quit the game before running
the helper and keep it closed until repair finishes; it never stops processes.

The helper also normalizes `doorstop_config.ini`. Doorstop resolves a relative
`target_assembly` and `dll_search_path_override` against the game folder, so a
value that already points into this folder from any absolute prefix — a manager
recording the install's own path, or a `Z:\` path copied from another machine —
becomes `BepInEx\core\BepInEx.Unity.IL2CPP.dll` and `BepInEx\core`, and the
stale `BepInEx/cache/chainloader_typeloader.dat`, which caches those absolute
paths, is deleted so the next launch rebuilds it. The original file is kept as
`doorstop_config.ini.bak` on the first rewrite; the other lines, the line
endings and the trailing newline are kept as they were. An absolute value that
points somewhere other than this game folder is left alone and reported instead
