The executable must have only an arm64 Mach-O slice and report `sysctl.proc_translated == 0`. An unknown-target subprocess must terminate with SIGABRT.

`task re:native-calls` adds a shipped-code oracle. Capture its controls once with `NATIVE_CALL_EXTRA='--capture-linux-host <ssh host> --linux-game-dir <game directory>'`; later runs reuse `.tools/native-calls/linux-controls.json` and a validated exact-body pcode cache. It resolves both `UnityEngine.Mathf.Clamp` overloads through the shipped Linux runtime, verifies overload types and input values, hashes the remote binary before/after capture, and requires that digest to equal the local ELF. On `GameAssembly.so` SHA-256 `a86fd434…5ba1`, the float overload is 17 B at RVA `0x4ab58f0`, and the int overload is 15 B at `0x4ab5910`. Typed native exports pass all three arguments plus a null `MethodInfo*`; six cases compare below-range, in-range and above-range results. Neither body uses substituted callees or modeled globals. These are shipped Unity math bodies, not a probe player or evidence of gameplay.

The command retains generated adapters, lifted IR/object, executable and reports under `.tools/native-calls/`. `NATIVE_CALL_EXTRA='--candidate-override 999'` must fail the comparison. The automated shipped-code test runs the same build and comparison when local binary/controls exist; missing artifacts skip it, but malformed controls or mismatched identity fail. The script itself fails when controls are absent.

Remaining exclusions: the fixed guest stack is a bounded probe allocation, not a guarded production stack; arbitrary recursion/large guest frames are not supported. Address relocation beyond the typeinfo words described in the next section, callback-pointer rewriting in image data, dynamic icall discovery, nonlocal exits other than exception unwinding, guest TLS and floating-point environment emulation are not supplied by these adapters; exception unwinding is described in the next section. Native runtime initialization and a Unity boot remain separate verification gates.

### Exception unwinding in lifted code

The shipped image statically contains libsupc++: `__gxx_personality_v0`, `__cxa_throw`, `__cxa_begin_catch`, `__cxa_end_catch` and `__cxa_rethrow` are image code and lift like any other body. Only the `_Unwind_*` family comes from libgcc_s, so that layer is what the lifted runtime supplies: `scripts/unwind_runtime/runtime.c` implements `_Unwind_RaiseException`, `_Unwind_Resume` and `_Unwind_Resume_or_Rethrow` as raw imports (`lift_abi.RawBinding`: a native function that receives the guest register file and owns the guest RSP), and `_Unwind_GetLanguageSpecificData`, `_Unwind_GetRegionStart`, `_Unwind_GetIPInfo`, `_Unwind_SetGR` and `_Unwind_SetIP` as ordinary typed imports. The personality routine is lifted code reached through `lift_dispatch`, so it must be registered with `lift_abi.generate(..., callable_bodies=[...])`.

The unwind walks the guest stack, not the native one. Raw pcode pushes real return addresses and callee-saved registers on the private guest stack, so `_Unwind_RaiseException` reads the shipped `.eh_frame` rows (built by `scripts/lift_unwind.py`) to find each frame's CFA and saved registers, runs phase one by calling the lifted personality with a search action, then phase two with a cleanup action and `_UA_HANDLER_FRAME` on the frame phase one chose. A phase-one walk that reaches a return address with no frame returns `_URC_END_OF_STACK`, so the shipped `__cxa_throw` code reaches `std::terminate` itself, and cleanups never run for an exception nothing catches. Raw pcode pushes Ghidra-space return addresses, a constant offset from the file addresses the tables use (`0x100000` on both the shipped image and the test fixture); `lift_unwind.address_bias` reads it from the pushed constants and refuses a body set whose calls disagree, and the runtime subtracts it before every lookup.

Landing is cooperative because native frames of lifted functions cannot be unwound by a guest walker. When the personality returns `_URC_INSTALL_CONTEXT` the runtime writes the target frame's registers into the register file (callee-saved registers from the frame rows, RAX and RDX from `_Unwind_SetGR`, RSP as the frame's value at the call) and records the landing pad and the owning frame's CFA in two register-file words (`pcode_lift.EH_PENDING_PAD`, `EH_PENDING_CFA`). With `lift_body(..., unwind=True, pads=...)` every call is followed by a check of the pad word. A frame that does not own the pending CFA returns at once, leaving the guest RSP alone; the owning frame clears the word and branches through a generated switch to the pad, which is reachable no other way. Bodies with no pads still get the check, because they sit between a throw and its handler, and a call-free body gets none. A tail call (a `call` followed by `ret` from an indirect or external jump) is not checked, since the frame is already gone. Every exported call checks the word after the guest returns and aborts if it is set, so a stray pending exception never escapes into native code.

Each export call owns its register file and guest stack, so an exception that is not caught inside one call ends in `std::terminate` there and never propagates into an outer native or lifted caller. A native import that re-enters an export is the same case: the inner call has its own register file, guest stack, pending words and TLS block, so an exception caught inside it leaves the outer frame's registers, cleanup order and pending state untouched (`tests/test_lift_unwind_exec.py` runs a lifted frame, a native import, a re-entered export that throws and catches, then a throw and cleanup in the outer frame), and one that is uncaught inside it terminates without running the outer frame's cleanups.

| Unsupported shape | Rejection |
| --- | --- |
| landing pad not an instruction start of the dumped body, or outside it | `EH_PAD_NOT_INSN`, `EH_PAD_OUTSIDE` (a hole on the lifted body) |
| pads given without `unwind=True` | `EH_PAD_WITHOUT_UNWIND` |
| call-frame instruction other than the ones the interpreter implements (expressions, `val_offset`, signed-factor forms, GNU negative offsets) | `EH_CFI` |
| CFA register other than RSP or RBP, or a saved register outside DWARF 0-16 | `EH_CFA_REGISTER`, `EH_SAVED_REGISTER` |
| body that calls something but has no frame description | `EH_NO_FDE` |
| LSDA frame with no resolvable personality, or one outside the allowed set | `EH_NO_PERSONALITY`, `EH_PERSONALITY_UNKNOWN` |
| explicit LPStart, a call-site table that overruns its bytes, or a CIE augmentation other than `zR` and `zPLR` | rejected while parsing |

`lift_unwind.render_c` refuses to emit tables for a set with any of these problems, so a module is never linked against tables that would silently stop an unwind at the wrong frame.

`task re:eh-coverage` interprets every frame of the il2cpp section this way and exits non-zero on any rejection. On `GameAssembly.so` SHA-256 `a86fd434…5ba1`: 196,137 bodies, all supported at the metadata level; 8,373 with an LSDA, all naming the image's own `__gxx_personality_v0`; 103,628 pad entries over 84,257 distinct pads, none outside its body; no call-frame instruction outside the implemented set. `--dump-pads` dumps pcode for every LSDA body in one Ghidra run (about 25 minutes, `GHIDRA_HEADLESS_MAXMEM=8G`, kept under `.tools/eh-coverage/pad-dump` and reused) and checks the dumped instructions: 8,373 of 8,373 LSDA bodies dumped, 84,257 distinct pads, none missing from its body's instruction starts. `lift_unwind.undumped_lsda_bodies` makes a body the dump dropped visible (`not_dumped`), so a short dump cannot pass. Lifting those 8,373 bodies with and without `unwind=True` gives identical per-body hole lists (ten bodies have holes in both modes, none gains one). On the 3,000-body seed-80 cache, which also holds bodies without an LSDA, the hole lists are identical too, with 47,050 post-call checks and about 2% more IR text; bodies without an LSDA differ from default lifting only by those checks, and the seed-81 and seed-82 draws were not re-scored.

Verified on native arm64 by `tests/test_lift_unwind_exec.py`, which lifts a freestanding C++ fixture compiled with `-fexceptions` (real LSDA, real landing pads, a minimal in-fixture `__cxa_*` runtime and personality) and runs it against the runtime: a caught throw with cleanups in destructor order, nested propagation through two frames that each clean up, a rethrow caught by an outer handler, callee-saved registers surviving a landing, the exception pointer delivered in RAX, an uncaught throw terminating with no cleanup run, and a stray pending word aborting the export. The returned guest RSP is checked on every call.

Guest TLS is the smallest slice the shipped libsupc++ needs: `lift_abi.Tls(get_addr, image)` makes every export call copy the image into a private 16-byte-aligned block, keep its address in the register-file word at `0x1F10`, and answer the lifted `__tls_get_addr` import with block plus the offset word of the `tls_index` it is given. `lift_abi.tls_image` builds the image from the `PT_TLS` segment (initialized data, then zeros to the memory size). It supports exactly one TLS module and the local-dynamic shape the shipped `__cxa_get_globals` uses, where the `tls_index` offset word is zero and only the module word is relocated (`DTPMOD64`, which the image does not apply and the adapter ignores). A general-dynamic access has a `DTPOFF64` relocation on the offset word that the image does not apply, so it reads as zero and would silently alias the first variable; `-ftls-model=local-dynamic` is what the fixture is built with. The block is not shared with the personality's own register file, nor with a re-entered export, so state in it is visible only to the frames of one export call. `tests/test_lift_unwind_exec.py` checks a zero-initialized and a `.tdata`-initialized variable, and that a second call starts from the initial image again.

The shipped libsupc++ runs lifted. `tests/test_lift_shipped_runtime.py` lifts the image's `__gxx_personality_v0`, `__cxa_throw`, `__cxa_rethrow`, `__cxa_begin_catch`, `__cxa_end_catch`, `__cxa_allocate_exception` and `__cxa_get_globals` (22 bodies, 2,950 bytes, found by `scripts/lift_runtime.py` following direct calls, the image's own PLT targets and nothing else), with no hole, together with the same fixture and no hand-written runtime, and gets the outcomes the fixture runtime gets: a caught throw with cleanups in destructor order, nested propagation, a rethrow, registers surviving a landing, and an uncaught throw ending in terminate with no cleanup run. The Ghidra dump of the closure is cached under `.tools/lift-runtime-cache` per image, body set and dump script. The fixture's `__cxa_*` entry points and personality are aliases (`lift_abi.generate(..., aliases=...)`) of the lifted image functions, and the fixture's CIE personality is remapped to the image's. What this shows is that the shipped runtime, not a stand-in, drives the guest unwinder; the expected outcomes are the C++ semantics of the fixture, not a run of the shipped x86-64 binary, so it is not yet a comparison against the shipped reference.

Bindings the shipped runtime needs, and what is deliberately not lifted. `_Unwind_RaiseException` and `_Unwind_Resume_or_Rethrow` are the raw guest unwinder; `_Unwind_GetLanguageSpecificData`, `GetRegionStart`, `GetIPInfo`, `SetGR` and `SetIP` are the typed imports; `malloc` and `free` are native; `_Unwind_DeleteException` is a native no-op, so `exception_cleanup` is never run and a thrown object is never freed. The terminate and unexpected entry points (`std::terminate`, `__cxa_call_terminate`, `__terminate`, `get_terminate` and friends) stay native, because the handler they call is a function pointer in unrelocated data: `std::terminate` aborts, which is the stated contract of terminating at the export boundary, and the two handler getters return null. `__gnu_cxx::__mutex::lock` and `__scoped_lock`'s destructor, which `__cxa_allocate_exception` reaches only when `malloc` fails, are native imports that abort. `__gxx_exception_cleanup` is reached only through a stored function pointer, uses `lock`-prefixed atomics the lifter rejects, and is bound to an aborting import. `lift_runtime.closure` reports the functions whose address is taken (`taken`) rather than lifting them, and every PLT import it cannot resolve to an image definition, so none is bound silently. The personality is called on its own register file, which carries no TLS block, and the generated `__tls_get_addr` aborts when it has none, so a shipped path through the personality that needs TLS fails loudly instead of reading another block. The tests do not reach one: `__cxa_begin_catch` and `__cxa_end_catch` run on the export's register file and do use the block.

Typed catch is matched by the image's own lifted `type_info` code, and only for typeinfo classes it was shown to run. The personality reads a type-table entry (pcrel, indirect), gets a typeinfo pointer, and calls through that typeinfo's vtable (`__is_pointer_p` and `__do_catch` from the personality, then the two `__do_upcast` overloads from `__do_catch` and `__do_upcast`: vtable slots 4, 6, 7 and 8). The lifted module keeps data sections as unrelocated file bytes, so `scripts/lift_reloc.py` applies the load-time relocations to exactly the words that path reads and rejects everything it cannot prove:

- `lift_reloc.plan_types` resolves, per LSDA, each nonzero type entry to its cell, the cell to a typeinfo object, the object to a vtable symbol that must be in `PROVEN_CLASSES` (`class_type_info`, `si_class_type_info`), the vtable's words, and the name word. A base-class pointer in a single-inheritance typeinfo recurses. `R_X86_64_RELATIVE` and `R_X86_64_64` against a defined symbol (or an undefined one the caller's linker map resolves) are handled; any other type, a missing or ambiguous relocation, a target outside every section, a vtable word with no relocation and a nonzero file value, an unproven class or a type-table encoding other than pcrel indirect sdata4 or sdata8 rejects the whole body (`EH_TYPEINFO_RELOC:<reason>`, `EH_TYPED_CATCH`, `EH_TTYPE_ENCODING`).
- A relocated word is a `Patch`. `pcode_lift.module_for(..., patches)` emits a patched section as a packed struct: a data target is `getelementptr (i8, ptr @sec_<target>, offset)`, so it is the host address of the target section plus offset, resolved by the linker; a code target is the file address as an `i64`, which is the value `lift_dispatch` switches on. A virtual call through a vtable word to a body that was not lifted therefore aborts in `lift_dispatch` instead of jumping anywhere.
- The unwinder hands the personality a host copy of the LSDA, so a type entry's pcrel distance is meaningless there. `lift_unwind.render_c(..., type_slots)` records each patched entry (offset in the LSDA copy, cell, 4 or 8 byte width) and a constructor in `runtime.c` rewrites the entry to the real host distance to the cell. The copy is therefore writable and the constructor aborts if a four-byte entry cannot hold the distance.
- `lift_unwind.build_entries(..., typed_ok=plan.proven)` accepts a typed catch only for an LSDA the plan proved; every other typed catch is still `EH_TYPED_CATCH`, and an exception specification is `EH_EXC_SPEC` regardless.
- A code address a lifted body materialises itself (a `lea` of a function) lowers to the host address of `@sub_X`, not the file address `lift_dispatch` switches on, so it disagrees with a dispatched call. Relocated data words do not use that path, so typed catch is unaffected; `tests/test_lift_code_address.py` holds it as a strict expected failure until callback pointers are handled.

Measured on the shipped image (`task re:eh-coverage`, `filters` and `type_words` keys). All 8,373 il2cpp-section LSDA bodies have exactly one typed entry, 325 distinct type-table cells in `.data`, every one `R_X86_64_RELATIVE` to the same typeinfo, the one for `Il2CppExceptionWrapper`, in `.data.rel.ro`. Its vtable word is `R_X86_64_64` against the image's own `class_type_info` vtable (88 bytes, 11 words), whose words are `R_X86_64_64` against symbols the image defines, and its name word is `R_X86_64_RELATIVE` into `.rodata`. A scan of the il2cpp section found 4,080 `__cxa_throw` call sites and every one passes that same typeinfo, so no other thrown type exists to reach an unrelocated typeinfo. The five `class_type_info` methods the typed path reaches (`__is_pointer_p`, `__do_catch`, both `__do_upcast` overloads and a shared helper) lift with no hole and call only `strcmp`, bound as a native import. The plan accepts 8,373 of 8,373 bodies with 337 patched words and 8,373 type slots, and rejects none. The image has no `pointer_type_info` vtable, so a pointer catch is rejected as an unproven class, and `vmi_class_type_info` is rejected the same way: its methods are lifted nowhere and no shipped LSDA names one. "Accepted" here is the typed-matching gate; it does not mean those bodies have been run.

`tests/test_lift_typed_catch.py` runs real RTTI natively on arm64: a fixture compiled with RTTI and no libstdc++ whose typeinfo objects have `R_X86_64_64` relocations against the two `type_info` vtables as undefined symbols. The test binds each name to the shipped image's own symbol, so the vtable that dispatches is the shipped one, lifted, together with the shipped libsupc++ (the fixture's stubs of it are replaced as in the shipped-runtime test). Cases: a derived class thrown and caught as its base (`si_class_type_info` through `class_type_info::__do_catch`), a catch of the exact derived type, a catch of the same class, a non-matching inner `catch (Other&)` skipped with two cleanups running before the outer `catch (Base&)`, a `catch (Derived&)` that must miss a thrown base and fall to `catch (...)`, and an unmatched typed catch that reaches terminate with no cleanup. The same sources linked against a stock x86-64 libstdc++ (`typed_reference_main.cpp`, outcomes in `tests/fixtures/lift/typed_reference.txt`) give identical return values, notes and exit status for all six cases. The stock libstdc++ is not the image's libsupc++, so this compares behavior, not code. The image's own libsupc++ gives the same outcomes natively: build the same two fixtures with `g++ -O1 typed.cpp typed_reference_main.cpp` (and `unwind.cpp` with `unwind_reference_main.cpp`) on an x86-64 Linux host, then run each mode with `LD_PRELOAD=<game dir>/GameAssembly.so`. `LD_DEBUG=bindings` shows `__cxa_throw`, `__cxa_allocate_exception`, `__cxa_begin_catch`, `__cxa_end_catch`, `__gxx_personality_v0` and the `class_type_info`, `si_class_type_info` and `vmi_class_type_info` vtables binding to `GameAssembly.so` (without the preload they bind to libstdc++), while `_Unwind_RaiseException` stays in libgcc_s, which is also what the image imports. All thirteen cases (six typed, seven unwind) give the same return values, notes and exit status as the stock build and as the lifted run, so the lifted shipped runtime is compared with the shipped runtime itself on every case the reference files list. This still compares fixture code, not a shipped il2cpp body with an LSDA. Breaking each mechanism fails the tests: dropping the patches from the module, skipping the type-slot rewrite, not reaching vtable slot 8, leaving the name word unrelocated, shifting a code address by one byte, and patching a four-byte slot as eight.

Not covered: typed catch for any class outside `PROVEN_CLASSES` (rejected above), the shipped il2cpp bodies themselves running typed catches (the plan accepts them and a fixture exercises the mechanism, but no shipped body with an LSDA has been run lifted), `exception_cleanup` and the terminate and unexpected handlers (native boundary above), forced unwinding, `_Unwind_Backtrace`, and exceptions raised by native (non-lifted) code, which have no C++ unwind tables in the guest walk and are rejected rather than emulated. A shipped managed method that throws deterministically, `System.ThrowHelper.ThrowInvalidOperationException_InvalidOperation_NoValue`, runs headless under the Linux runtime and reports a thrown exception through `il2cpp_runtime_invoke`.

`scripts/eh_oracle.py` records what a shipped method throws on the Linux runtime: it runs `scripts/native_bind_probe/host.c` headless over SSH with `--expect-throw`, which treats a throw from `il2cpp_runtime_invoke` as the expected outcome and records the exception class and `il2cpp_format_exception` message instead of failing. `uv run scripts/eh_oracle.py --host <ssh host> --linux-game-dir <dir>` writes `.tools/eh-oracle/oracles.json`. The only oracle so far is `System.ThrowHelper::ThrowInvalidOperationException_InvalidOperation_NoValue` (mscorlib, static, no arguments), which throws `System.InvalidOperationException` with message "Nullable object must have a value.". It is a reference outcome for a throw, not yet a comparison: lifting it needs the il2cpp runtime state that builds the managed exception. No shipped method with a catch, a finally or a rethrow around a throwing call has been identified, so cleanup order, nested propagation and rethrow have no shipped oracle. The host tests run against a stub runtime (`tests/fixtures/bind/il2cpp_stub.c`).

### Cross-checking the lift against an external reconstruction

`~/git/open_bonk` (OpenBonk) reconstructs the same game's managed sources to ship an Android player. Its `README.md` states what it is: "The code is a reconstruction, not a byte-for-byte decompilation." Two of its checks are worth reading against the lift because they pin the binary instead of trusting a build label, and both name the SHA-256 this repository pins, `a86fd434…5ba1`: `analysis/port-inventory/check-original-noimplementation.js` and `analysis/port-inventory/compare-item-factory.js`. The rest of that tree was derived from a Windows `GameAssembly.dll` and an Android APK, so agreement has to be established address by address rather than assumed.

The reconstruction cannot be joined to a lifted body by name. Inside the 47 MB `il2cpp` section the ELF's `.symtab` defines exactly two symbols, `__start_il2cpp` and `__stop_il2cpp`; every other entry is an import or a libc/libm export. `MelonLoader/Il2CppAssemblies/*.dll` and `DummyDll/*.dll` are interop stubs with no bodies. `compare-item-factory.js` shows where its own names come from: `analysis/il2cpp/script.json` (Il2CppDumper's metadata address-to-name map for the Linux build), `analysis/il2cpp/elf-relocations.txt` for the `R_X86_64_RELATIVE` words that point code at `*_TypeInfo` structs, and a disassembly kept in `analysis/recovered/`. None of those three is in the checkout, and this repository's `dump.cs` is the Windows dump, whose RVAs do not address the Linux image. So an address-to-name join needs Il2CppDumper over the pinned Linux pair: `GameAssembly.so` plus the depot's `_Data/il2cpp_data/Metadata/global-metadata.dat`, which is not on this machine.

Addresses alone allow one reconciliation pass. Over every hex literal that tree publishes (`analysis/`, `docs/`, the Java check harnesses), 58 distinct values, classified against the pinned image's executable sections — `.init` at `0x1d35000`, `.text` from `0x1d35040` for `0x42d941`, `il2cpp` from `0x2162990` to `0x4e26440`, `.plt` from `0x4e26440` — and against the scored body map:

| Class | Count | What it is |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Body start in the scored image | 10 | All clean. Two are section-entry constants their scripts define (`0x1d35000`, `0x2162990`); the other eight are cited methods: `0x2164cb0`, `0x2164d40`, `0x22c61b0`, `0x22c65c0`, `0x22c6b40`, `0x22c6b60`, `0x22c6c90`, `0x22cdb90` |
| Interior to a scored body | 9 | Jump-table case targets: four inside `0x227e020`, four inside `0x233d640`, `0x2cc3ab0` inside `0x2cc3560` |
| Below `0x1d35000` | 24 | Not code here — `.rodata` or `.eh_frame`. Another build's RVAs: their `docs/BUILDING.md` cites "the local beta IL2CPP library" with SHA-256 `3ba3…` |
| Outside every executable section | 15 | Data addresses (`0x4e2d210`, `0x515e460`) and magic constants (`0xfab11baf`, `0x11223344`, `0x80000000`) |

No cited address falls inside one of the 148 blocked bodies.

The one place both sides derive the same fact from the bytes is the item factory's switch. Body `0x227e020` (4,521 B, 1,000 instructions, clean) recovers one jump table and audits none of it. The 88 little-endian i32 entries at `0xb6f7c8` in `.rodata` give 86 distinct targets spanning `0x227e465` to `0x227f12e`, every one inside the body, with three duplicates: indices 69, 79 and 81 all target `0x227e465`. `check-original-noimplementation.js` asserts those same three indices against that same target and names the shared case `ItemNoImplementation`, reached by `EItem.CageKey`, `EItem.GoldenRing` and `EItem.CryptKey`. Ghidra's FlowOverride for the site at `0x227e463` reports 87 cases — the byte set plus `0x227f152`, which is also inside the body — and that single disagreement between recovered flow and table bytes is what holds `scripts/lift_tables.py` at zero audited tables for the body. Two independent decodes agree on the content and on containment; the difference is one extra case, and the reason the audit is conservative is on the record.

What the reconstruction cannot contribute. It is not an oracle for a lowering: it was written to make a Unity player run, its `Assembly-CSharp` tree carries `return null;` stubs that its own audits exist to enumerate, and its scripts disclaim their own reach ("Class selection only, not item behavior", "detects names, not code identity, authorship or distribution rights"). It says nothing about the 148 blocked bodies, which turn on a frame fact the dump does not contain. What a lowered operation means stays settled by the executed differential tests and by Ghidra's own verification. That tree is research context, not a dependency: it is a reconstruction of the same proprietary game, and no code or asset from it belongs in this repository or in a shipped mod.

### OpenBonk reconstruction on native macOS

The reconstructed-source alternative is no longer a from-scratch proposal. A private experiment with OpenBonk revision `7d7fd684`, the local Windows game data, AssetRipper 2.0.0 mac-arm64 and Unity 2023.2.22f1 built and executed a macOS IL2CPP player on Apple silicon. This is reconstructed C# compiled by Unity, **not** the shipped x86 code lifted by `pcode_lift.py`, and it establishes no equivalence to the shipped game.

The experiment keeps the export, assembled workspace, app bundles, scripts, screenshots and logs outside both source checkouts. Product/company and bundle identity are changed before the first Unity import so the player uses a separate research profile; existing game saves are neither imported nor reset. All game-derived outputs remain private. The input binary/metadata hashes and exact source revision belong in the private experiment manifest, not in a distributable game package.

Preparation reuses OpenBonk's export and GUID-aware overlay assembly, managed-plugin normalization, offline Steam stub, input-axis preparation, readable grass mesh, package-script remapping and skybox restoration. The overlay required 69 GUID mappings across 623 files; package-script repair changed 7,986 references in 1,015 files. The Windows export had no supported Linux texture-case-collision pair, so that preparation was skipped rather than guessed. The macOS Unity Mono directory differs from the Linux build scripts' layout.

Several failures were integration work rather than architecture barriers:

- Exported Windows `StreamingAssets/aa` catalogs and link data conflict with Unity's newly generated OSX Addressables. Preserve the exported files outside the workspace and build new platform data.
- Exported post-processing resources still point to unusable compute shaders. The existing restoration step reconnects 24 raster and 12 compute shaders from the matching Unity package.
- Minimal stripping retains an unused exported Discord import which fails in UnityLinker. Low stripping passed; Discord integration was not implemented or verified.
- The initial player reaches `MainMenu` but has missing localization keys. Reading the preserved Windows localization bundles with the existing exporter and changing the rebuilder's target to StandaloneOSX recovered 17 locales, 867 tables and 19,157 entries. The existing GUID repair restored 51 collection GUIDs across 919 files; collection, GUID and English/Spanish default-description checks passed.
- Map-generation startup is guarded by `UNITY_ANDROID && !UNITY_EDITOR`. A workspace-only OSX branch starts the existing recovered map coroutine, not the older substitute-arena bootstrap. The existing prepared-scene step supplies tile references and removes unsupported plugin components.
- Reloading the experiment's own profile initially throws in Newtonsoft's `JsonArrayContract.CreateWrapper`, whose reflected constructor is missing. Explicit `AotHelper` coverage for string collections and the progression dictionaries removes that failure without resetting the profile.
- Prepared scenes remove unsupported Rewired components and leave a disabled standard input module. The private desktop adapter must discover loaded EventSystems including persistent objects, add a standard module if absent, and enable it without changing existing bindings. Scanning only the newly loaded scene misses the active persistent EventSystem. Installation-only regression coverage missed this lifecycle; a Play Mode regression now reproduces the persistent-before-callback ordering.

The resulting bundle is universal x86_64/arm64 despite requesting arm64 in the build entry point. Select and measure the running slice rather than calling the artifact arm64-only:

```sh
arch -arm64 "<experiment>/OpenBonkMacResearch.app/Contents/MacOS/OpenBonkMacResearch20261116" \
  -research-smoke -research-drive -research-output "<experiment>/smoke" \
  -logFile "<experiment>/player.log" -screen-fullscreen 0
sample <player-pid> 1 -file "<experiment>/native-process.txt"
```

Those research switches belong to a workspace-only smoke component, not to upstream OpenBonk or the shipped game. The component captures frames, invokes existing menu/map handlers when explicitly requested, and exits after a bounded run. macOS `sample` reports `Code Type: ARM64`; the player reports `OSXPlayer`, Metal and the Apple M4 Max. A captured run renders Fox in Forest with terrain, HUD, minimap and timer, defeats 15 enemies, and reaches the level-one upgrade screen. The previously failing profile reload succeeds, and that smoke log contains no gameplay exception. The final build still reports three errors despite a successful build result; it is not a clean production pipeline.

Separate focus-guarded macOS input runs exercise W movement and Space with changed gameplay views and ongoing combat. A diagnostic baseline reproduces blocked upgrade selection with actual focused mouse-down/up at the measured offer center and raycasts resolving to that offer, followed by Down and Enter received by Unity. The offer is enabled/interactable and already has a serialized `SelectUpgrade` listener, but `EventSystem.currentInputModule` is null and no button click is dispatched. Do not fill the empty `UpgradeButton.OnClick` as a workaround: the existing serialized listener is the normal award path, and duplicating it risks double awards. Successful event posting alone is not successful UI interaction.

The private probe is read-only apart from diagnostic output and a logging-only click listener. Mouse targeting uses the offer's actual screen-space center and verifies the resulting Unity position/raycast; the saved video settings can override a windowed launch request, requiring centered-letterbox correction rather than a guessed title-bar offset.

#### Desktop upgrade-input handoff

The scene-only candidate's bounded native run completes normally but **still cannot select an upgrade**. Its diagnostic `availableModule=StandaloneInputModule:False:True` means the module exists, is disabled and reports supported; `currentInputModule` remains null. Focused mouse input reaches the offered button, but no `MAC_UPGRADE_CLICK` is recorded, all three tracked upgrade levels remain zero, and `MyTime.paused=True` with the stage timer unchanged. The process exits with `MAC_RESEARCH_COMPLETE` and driver status 0, with no gameplay exception: normal exit is not successful selection. The combined driver never reaches its subsequent-offer keyboard action, so this run proves neither keyboard selection nor a subsequent level-up.

The retained private experiment directory is named `openbonk-macos-spike-20261116`. Read its `EXPERIMENT.md` and inspect the current scripts before rerunning; early notes there describe intermediate candidates, not necessarily working results.

| Artifact relative to the experiment | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `working-build-07.app`, `profile-before-upgrade-task/` | Preserved pre-investigation app and isolated-profile backup; do not reset the profile |
| `diagnostic-build-08.app`, `upgrade-baseline-04/player.log` | Unchanged selection logic with a verified focused click/raycast and received Down/Enter, but no active input module |
| `MacResearchCompatibility.cs`, `MacUpgradeInputCheck.cs` | Candidate scene-load adapter and editor-only installation regression; matching copies are under the workspace's Scripts and Editor directories |
| `upgrade-input-test-red.log`, `upgrade-input-test-green.log` | Failing-before/passing-after editor evidence, not native award evidence |
| `MacUpgradeProbe.cs`, `upgrade-input.swift` | Read-only runtime diagnostics and focus-guarded OS input driver; neither is the mod's file-queue harness |
| `build-10.log`, `upgrade-fixed-01/`, `upgrade-fixed-01-driver.log`, `upgrade-fixed-01-native.txt` | Candidate build, failed native validation, driver outcome and measured ARM64 process |
| `diagnostic-build-11.app`, `upgrade-lifecycle-01/` | Callback and object-identity evidence establishing missed persistent EventSystem coverage |
| `MacUpgradeInputLifecycleCheck.cs`, `upgrade-lifecycle-test-red.log`, `upgrade-lifecycle-test-green.log` | Play Mode regression for a disabled EventSystem moved to persistent storage before scene-loaded installation |
| `build-12.log`, `upgrade-fixed-02/`, `upgrade-fixed-02-native.txt` | Revised adapter's native mouse award/resume evidence and ARM64 measurement; player dies before the subsequent keyboard offer |
| `upgrade-fixed-03/`, `upgrade-sequence.swift` | Same-profile keyboard-first/subsequent-mouse evidence and movement-aware OS driver |
| `MacUpgradeProbeCheck.cs`, `upgrade-probe-test-red.log`, `upgrade-probe-test-green.log` | Regression for dropping stale inventory observations after returning to the menu |
| `build-13.log`, `upgrade-fixed-04/`, `upgrade-fixed-04-native.txt` | Clean same-profile ARM64 mouse-first/subsequent-keyboard validation with the probe guard |

The build entry point refuses an existing output app. Preserve each app under a new name before rebuilding, use a fresh run-output directory, and run only one player against the isolated profile at a time. From the directory containing the private driver, a bounded combined check is:

```sh
swift upgrade-input.swift "<experiment>" "<new-run-directory-name>" both
```

The driver passes `-research-upgrades` to enable the probe and `-research-long` for an approximately three-minute smoke deadline. It attempts a measured first-offer mouse click, then waits for a later offer before sending Down/Enter. Verify those actions actually occurred in the driver and player logs; a missing target, death before offers or no subsequent offer is an unmet test precondition, not a passed input path.

Callback diagnostics establish a coverage failure, not a later disabling write. `AlwaysManager.Awake` moves its hierarchy into `DontDestroyOnLoad` before activating the EventSystem. By the MainMenu scene-loaded callback, the current EventSystem and its disabled module are already outside that scene's roots. The next scene load enables a different, inactive scene EventSystem while leaving the current persistent one disabled. Discovering all loaded EventSystems, including inactive objects, covers both cases without a per-frame re-enable.

The lifecycle regression was run failing before changing discovery, then passing afterward. It enters real editor Play Mode, moves a disabled module into persistent storage before invoking installation, and checks enabled state, existing submit-binding preservation and idempotence. The original missing-module/default-axis regression also still passes.

The revised adapter's native ARM64 run dispatches one measured mouse click on Firestaff, raises its level from 1 to 2 while the other tracked offers remain at zero, closes the offers and resumes normal gameplay. The stage timer advances from 29.23 to 38.94 seconds before ordinary player death.

A same-profile restart uses `upgrade-sequence.swift` to move between offers using OS keys only. Down visibly focuses the second offer (including its outline and hover scale), and Enter awards Projectile Speed Tome once, closes the screen and resumes gameplay. A subsequent level-up accepts one mouse click for Golden Tome, again awarding once and resuming. That run later returns from the death screen to MainMenu, exposing stale inventory reads in the diagnostic probe rather than the selection handler. A separate failing-before/passing-after editor regression covers dropping inventory observations when the run is gone.

The clean rerun with that probe guard loads the same isolated profile without a reset, runs as ARM64 and completes normally with no logged exceptions. Mouse awards Damage Tome 0 → 1 at stage time 17.35; Down visibly focuses Firestaff on the next offer, and Enter awards it 1 → 2 at 35.30. Each dispatch occurs once, unselected tracked levels stay unchanged, offers close and gameplay resumes. Stage time reaches 57.86 and a third offer opens; the driver intentionally sends no selection to that offer. This is not a stuck input state.

Reproduce the movement-aware sequence from the private experiment directory:

```sh
swift upgrade-sequence.swift "<experiment>" "<new-run-directory-name>" mouse-first
```

Use `keyboard-first` to reverse the two selection paths. Both modes retain normal damage, use no award handler or pause override, and run to the smoke deadline. Ordinary death before another offer remains an unmet precondition, not a successful second selection.

Do not invoke the selection handler directly or force an unpause as input verification. A working result must show the intended upgrade's level increasing exactly once, visible keyboard focus where applicable, normal screen closure/resume, a subsequent level-up and restart against the same isolated profile.

This is a useful native-execution and short-combat result, not a full-run, physical-controller, audio, save-parity or rendering-parity result. The source contains approximations and remaining stubs, and the captures show visual defects. It does not replace the shipped-build differential oracle or retire the lifted-runtime route. The next decision should compare measured input, save/restart, longer-run and visual behavior against the frozen shipped game before adopting the reconstruction as a verification platform.

### Effort / de-risking

The Il2Cpp runtime bridge is the make-or-break; per-function reconstruction is tedious but bounded. The shader blocker that decided B's shape is now measured and scriptable ("Shader translation pass" above), the Steamworks question is answered by static analysis and offline runs against the real shim ("Steamworks and the offline gate"), and the loader question is measured too: the published macOS bundle generates `BepInEx/interop` and `LogOutput.log` under Rosetta once the app-bundle paths are where it probes and dyld is told where to find its bare-name `GameAssembly` load ("Companion checks" above). The by-symbol question is now measured as well, and it came back yes: a loader-free native host binds the runtime, invokes game-authored `Assembly-CSharp` methods by name and patches an exported `il2cpp_*` function with a jump it wrote itself ("Binding the Il2Cpp runtime without a mod loader" above), so the chainloader's `il2cpp_runtime_invoke` trigger is a gate a recomp can walk around rather than a dependency it must satisfy. So is the step that gated the route choice: loading and shimming the shipped Linux ELF on macOS scores feasible on all four legs with every reachable import carrying a named answer ("Loading and shimming the Linux ELF runtime" above), which retires the clause that made Route C the fallback. What is left of B is therefore construction rather than unknowns: the 66-symbol compat layer, the 49-program shader tail (`task re:shaders-metal` reports it), and the translate-the-`.so`-into-a-dylib step itself, whose contract is now pinned down — preserve the 240 `il2cpp_*` exports, apply 466,320 base-relative fixups before `DT_INIT` and the 21 init hooks, then honor `PT_GNU_RELRO`. The smallest first steps remain (1) get one plugin to load under the macOS loader, which needs either a working detour provider or a host that does not gate on that trigger — and the provider is swappable by config plus one `libfunchook.dylib` in that private directory, so a build of libfunchook for macOS x86_64 is the cheaper of the two; (2) put that measurement to work as a native host that boots a recomp's own code into the game process. The standing gate that used to sit in front of all of this — whether running under CrossOver/Wine was itself the bottleneck, never measured — is retired: the port is wanted because Wine is not an acceptable end state, not because it is slow.

### Real keyboard text

Under plain Wine on macOS (Wine Devel 11) the game receives real key state but no text: `Input.GetKey` and `Input.GetKeyDown` report W/A/S/D, tool hotkeys and Backspace, while `Input.inputString` stays empty for every key. `TextFieldModel.Type` reads characters, Backspace (`\b`) and Return only from the text string, so a text field never saw any of them and typing in the level-name and palette search boxes did nothing. An earlier retest that found typing working on the same setup is unexplained. Return does not arrive as a key-down at all, because Wine shows its window title bar instead, so Enter cannot save a level there; use the Save button. Keypad Enter was not tried because the test keyboard had no numpad. CrossOver and Windows were not compared.

`EditorInput.InputString` therefore uses the real `Input.inputString` when it is non-empty and otherwise rebuilds text from this frame's key-downs through `KeyTextMapper` (`key_text_mapper.cs`, Unity-free and unit tested). The mapper assumes a US keyboard layout: letters, digits, the punctuation keys, Space and the keypad, with Shift selecting the upper character, and Backspace and Return mapped to `\b` and `\n`. A held key does not repeat, because only key-downs are read. On a runtime that delivers `inputString`, the fallback never runs.

With the dev harness enabled and the editor active, `EditorInput.LogRealTextInput` writes a `RealInput:` line to `LogOutput.log` for every frame in which the real keyboard produced text or a Backspace or Return key-down. A line carries the escaped `Input.inputString`, its character codes, the key-downs, and `focused=true|false` for whether an editor text field had focus. It reads `UnityEngine.Input` only and never the injected queue, so harness-typed text and the key-down fallback do not appear as text. To use it, relaunch, click the game window and a text box, press keys on the real keyboard, and read the log with `log_tail` and the pattern `RealInput`. No line for a keypress means the game never received it. A line with `focused=false` means a key arrived but no field had focus. A line with `focused=true` and an unchanged box means the field received the input and did not take it.
